\textstyle{\varphi^{*}\Omega^{1}_{Y}(\log B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{X/Y}(\log D^{h})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}

Therefore we get the following lemma:

Lemma 3.13
.

Under the above assumptions there exist canonical short exact sequences:

0\to{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\to{\bigoplus}{\mathcal{O}}_{D^{h}_{i}}\to 0

and

0\to\varphi^{*}\Omega^{1}_{Y}(\log B)\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\to 0.

For future reference we also note the following easy lemma:

Lemma 3.14
.

Let

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus m}\to{\mathbb{L}}\to T_{X}(-\log D)\to 0

be a short exact sequence of Lie algebroids
and let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a line bundle on
X
. Then we have an induced short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}({\mathcal{L}})\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus m}\to 0.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}({\mathcal{L}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(cf. Subsection

).

3.8
Hyperplane subbundles in projective bundles

Let us consider the following situation. Let

Y
be a smooth projective variety and let
\pi:X={\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})\to Y
be a projectivization of a vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
of rank
m+1
. Let us set
{\mathcal{L}}:={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})}(1)
. In
X
we consider a smooth divisor
D
, which is also a projective bundle over
Y
. Assume that over some point
y\in Y
we have
(X_{y},D_{y})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{m},H)
, where
H
is a hyperplane.
Then
{\mathcal{L}}(-D)|_{X_{y}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{y}}
and since
{\mathop{\rm Pic\,}}{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq{\mathop{\rm Pic\,}}Y\times{\mathbb{Z}}
, there exists some line bundle
{\mathcal{M}}
on
Y
such that

{\mathcal{L}}(-D)\simeq\pi^{*}{\mathcal{M}}.

Let us set
{\mathcal{G}}:=\pi_{*}({\mathcal{L}}|_{D})
. This is a vector bundle and
(D,{\mathcal{L}}|_{D})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{G}}),{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{G}})}(1))
.
By the projection formula we have
\pi_{*}({\mathcal{L}}(-D))={\mathcal{M}}
and
R^{1}\pi_{*}({\mathcal{L}}(-D))=0
.
Pushing down the standard short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{L}}(-D)\to{\mathcal{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}}|_{D}\to 0,

we get the short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{G}}\to 0.

In particular, we have
\det{\mathcal{G}}\simeq\det{\mathcal{E}}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}^{-1}
.

Lemma 3.15
.

\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log D)
is canonically isomorphic to
\pi^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)
.

Proof.

Since
\pi
is a projective bundle, we have

\pi_{*}(\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(1))=0
and
R^{1}\pi_{*}(\Omega_{Z/T}^{1}(1))=0
.
We have a short exact sequence

0\to\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(1)\to(\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log D))(1)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{D}(1)\to 0,

where the first non-zero map is the canonical inclusion and the second one is the relative Poincaré residue.
Pushing the above sequence down to
Y
, we get a canonical isomorphism

\pi_{*}((\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log{D}))(1))\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\simeq}\pi_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{D}(1))={\mathcal{G}}.

Let us recall that if
H
is a hyperplane in
{\mathbb{P}}^{m}
then
(\Omega_{{\mathbb{P}}^{m}}^{1}(\log H))(1)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{m}}^{\oplus m}
. So the relative evaluation map

\pi^{*}\pi_{*}((\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log{D}))(1))\longrightarrow(\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log{D}))(1)

is an isomorphism on the fibers of
\pi
and hence it is an isomorphism. This shows that
the relative logarithmic cotangent bundle
\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log{D})
is canonically isomorphic to
\pi^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)
.
∎

In the following lemma we use the notation from Subsection
3.7
.

Lemma 3.16
.

We have a canonical short exact sequence

0\to\pi^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{{{\mathcal{M}}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\to\pi^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)\to 0.

Proof.

In the short exact sequence

0\to\Omega_{X}^{1}\to\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log{D})\to{\mathcal{O}}_{D}\to 0,

the connecting map
H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{D})\to H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})
maps
1
to
c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}({D}))
. So the image of
c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}({D}))=c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})-c_{1}(\pi^{*}{\mathcal{M}})
in
H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log{D}))
vanishes. This shows that
c_{1}^{\log}({\mathcal{L}})=c_{1}^{\log}(\pi^{*}{\mathcal{M}})
and hence
the logarithmic Atiyah extensions of
{\mathcal{L}}
and
\pi^{*}{\mathcal{M}}
for the pair
(X,D)
are isomorphic.
By naturality of the first Chern class
\pi^{*}c_{1}({\mathcal{M}})\in H^{1}(X,\pi^{*}\Omega_{Y}^{1})
is mapped to
c_{1}(\pi^{*}{\mathcal{M}})\in H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})
. Thus we get the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi^{*}\Omega_{Y}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

Hence by Lemma
3.15
we have the short exact sequence

0\to\pi^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{{{\mathcal{M}}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\to\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log{D})\simeq\pi^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)\to 0.

∎

Remark 3.17
.

Let us point out that the image of

\pi^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}
is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, so it gives a canonical splitting of the relative logarithmic Atiyah extension

0\to\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log{D})\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to 0.

Since
\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log{D})\simeq\pi^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)
, this splitting can be also given by the relative evaluation map

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\simeq\pi^{*}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}.

This follows from the fact that
\pi^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}
factors through
\pi^{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{M}}}\to\pi^{*}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/Y}
.

4
Contact structures on special Lie algebroids

In this section we define a semi-contact structure on an snc pair and classify such structures in case
b_{2}=1
. Then we study the Kodaira dimension of semi-contact snc pairs and prove some result on existence of lines.

4.1
Contact snc pairs and special Lie algebroids

Let
(X,D)
be an snc pair.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a line bundle on
X
.
A
(generically)
{\mathcal{L}}
-valued contact structure
on
(X,D)
is a (generically)
{\mathcal{L}}
-valued contact structure on the canonical Lie algebroid
T_{X}(-\log D)
. We say that
(X,D,{\mathcal{L}})
is

(generically) contact
if
(X,D)
is an snc pair, and
there exists a (generically)
{\mathcal{L}}
-valued contact structure on
(X,D)
.
Sometimes we say that
(X,D)
is
(generically) contact
if there exists a line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}
on
X
such that
(X,D,{\mathcal{L}})
is (generically) contact.

Note that if
\theta
is a generically
{\mathcal{L}}
-valued contact structure on
(X,D)

then there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset
V\subset X
such that
\theta|_{V}
is an
{\mathcal{L}}|_{V}
-valued contact structure on
V
(it is sufficient to take
V=U\backslash{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D
for
U
in the definition). So
(X,D)
is generically contact if and only if
X
is generically contact. Clearly, a smooth projective variety which is birational to a generically contact variety is also generically contact.

Note that
[
BKK22
, Section 1]
uses a different definition of a generically contact variety in which the map
T_{X}\to{\mathcal{L}}
is surjective. This notion strongly restricts possible varieties and it depends on the choice of a birational model.

Let
(X,D)
be a projective snc pair. We have on
X
a stratification induced by the components of the divisor
D
.
To describe a stratum, we write
D=\sum_{i\in I}D_{i}
as a sum of irreducible divisors and we fix
J\subset I
.
Then a stratum
Z
is an irreducible component of
D_{J}
. In the following we write
D^{Z}
for the divisor
D^{J}|_{Z}
. To make things more precise we write
\imath
for the closed embedding
Z\hookrightarrow X
.
An important role in this paper is played by the following Lie algebroid structure induced on
Z
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
m=\dim X-\dim Z
. Then we have a canonical Lie algebroid structure on
\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)
and a canonical central extension

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}^{\oplus m}\to\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)\to T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})\to 0

of Lie algebroids.

Proof.

Exactness of the above sequence as a sequence of
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
-modules follows from standard short exact sequences for logarithmic bundles by taking duals. For example, the first non-zero map in the sequence is dual to the direct sum of restrictions to
Z
of Poincaré residues
\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log D)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{i}}
for
i\in J
. The anchor map for
\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)
is given by the composition
\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)\to T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})\hookrightarrow T_{Z}
. The Lie bracket for

\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)={\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\otimes_{\imath^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}\imath^{-1}T_{X}(-\log D)

is given by the formula

[f_{1}\otimes\delta_{1},f_{2}\otimes\delta_{2}]=1\otimes[\tilde{f}_{1}\delta_{1},\tilde{f}_{2}\delta_{2}],

where
f_{1},f_{2}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
,
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}\in T_{X}(-\log D)
and
\tilde{f}_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
is a lift of
f_{i}
for
i=1,2
. It is easy to check that the formula does not depend on the choice of lifts (here it is convenient to interpret the Lie bracket as the standard bracket in the ring of differential operators and use the fact that if
f\in I_{Z}
then
\delta(f)\in I_{Z}
for
\delta\in T_{X}(-\log D)
). This gives rise to the required Lie algebroid structure.
It is also clear from the formula, that the map
\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)\to T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})
is a map of Lie algebroids.
So the anchor map on the kernel is trivial and hence the corresponding Lie algebra structure is
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
-linear.
Now let us choose local (algebraic) coordinates
(x_{1},...,x_{n})
so that
Z
is given by
x_{1}=...=x_{m}=0
. Then
T_{X}(-\log D)

has a local basis
x_{1}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}},...,x_{m}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{m}},\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{m+1}},...,\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{n}}
and the kernel of
\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)\to T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})

is generated as an
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
-module by
1\otimes\left(x_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\right)
for
i=1...,m
. Since
\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}},\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}\right]=0
, we have

1\otimes\left[x_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}},\sum_{j\leq m}f_{j}x_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}+\sum_{j>m}f_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}\right]=\sum_{j\leq m}x_{i}\frac{\partial f_{j}}{\partial x_{i}}\otimes x_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}+\sum_{j>m}x_{i}\frac{\partial f_{j}}{\partial x_{i}}\otimes\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}=0

for all
1\leq i\leq m
.
So the Lie bracket on the kernel is trivial and
\left\{1\otimes\left(x_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\right)\right\}_{i=1,..,m}

define central sections of
\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)
.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

The above lemma allows us to upgrade
\imath:Z\hookrightarrow X
to a morphism of D-schemes

(Z,{\mathcal{D}}_{T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})})\to(X,{\mathcal{D}}_{T_{X}(-\log D)})

in the sense of
[
BB93
, 1.4.2]
.

Remark 4.4
.

One can easily check that the class of the extension from Lemma
4.2

is given by

\bigoplus_{i\in J}c_{1}^{\log}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(D_{i}))\in{\mathop{{\rm Ext\,}}}^{1}(T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z}),{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}^{\oplus m})=H^{1}(Z,\Omega_{Z}^{1}(\log D^{Z}))^{\oplus m}.

Note that if
(X,D,{\mathcal{L}})
is a contact projective log variety then for any
Z
as in the lemma, the restriction

\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)\to\imath^{*}{\mathcal{L}}
is a contact structure on the above Lie algebroid
\imath^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)
. This allows us to study logarithmic contact structures by induction on the dimension of the underlying variety. It also motivates the following definition of contact structures on some Lie algebroids generalizing Picard Lie algebroids (see
[
BB93
, Definition 2.1.3]
).

Definition 4.5
.

Let
(X,D)
be a projective snc pair.

1.

A
special Lie algebroid
on
(X,D)
is a Lie algebroid
{\mathbb{L}}
together with an abelian extension

0\to{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{L}}\to T_{X}(-\log D)\to 0

of Lie algebroids. In the following we often omit the extension in the notation.

2.

A special Lie algebroid
{\mathbb{L}}
on
(X,D)
is called
super-special
if the corresponding extension is central.

3.

A
special (generically) contact Lie algebroid
on
(X,D)
is a triple
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}},{\mathbb{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}})
consisting of a special Lie algebroid
{\mathbb{L}}
, a line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}
and a (generically)
{\mathcal{L}}
-valued contact structure
{\mathbb{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}}
. In the following we omit the contact structure in the notation.

4.

We say that
(X,D)
is
(generically) semi-contact of rank
(2r+1)
if there exists a special (generically) contact Lie algebroid
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
on
(X,D)
with
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}{\mathbb{L}}=2r+1
.

The proof of the following lemma is similar to that of Lemma
4.2
and we leave it to the reader.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
be a special contact Lie algebroid on
(X,D)
. Then for any irreducible stratum
Z
of the stratification induced by
D
, the restriction
({\mathbb{L}}|_{Z},{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z})
is a special contact Lie algebroid on
(Z,D^{Z})
.

4.2
Bounds on existence of a semi-contact structure

The following result shows that varieties admitting a semi-contact structure are quite rare.

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
be a special contact Lie algebroid of rank
(2r+1)
on
(X,D)
. If
K_{X}+D
is not nef then
\dim X\geq r
and if equality holds then
D=0
. If moreover
b_{2}(X)=1
then one of the following cases occurs:

1.

\dim X=2r+1
and
(X,D)
is a contact log Fano variety.

2.

For some
0\leq s\leq r
we have
(X,{\mathcal{L}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r+s},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1))
,
D
is an arrangement of
s
hyperplanes in a general position and
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}^{\oplus(r+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}
. Moreover, we have
either
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}^{\oplus r}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}
or
s=0
and
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}^{\oplus(r+1)}\oplus T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}
.

Proof.

Let us write
\dim X=r+s
for some
s\leq r+1
. By assumption there exists a short exact sequence of Lie algebroids

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r+1-s)}\to{\mathbb{L}}\to T_{X}(-\log D)\to 0

(2)

So existence of an
{\mathcal{L}}
-valued contact structure on
{\mathbb{L}}
implies that
-(K_{X}+D)=(r+1)c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
. If
K_{X}+D
is not nef then there exists a good log contraction
\varphi
of
(X,D)
. So by Lemma
1.6
we can find a rational curve
C
such that

0<-(K_{X}+D)\cdot C=(r+1){\mathcal{L}}\cdot C\leq\dim X+1=r+s+1.

This shows that

s\geq 0
. If
s=0
then the same lemma implies that
D=0
.

Now assume that
b_{2}(X)=1
. Then
-(K_{X}+D)
is ample and hence
{\mathcal{L}}
is ample.
For small
\epsilon>0
the divisor
-(K_{X}+D)+\epsilon D
is also ample.
So by the Kawamata–Viehweg vanishing theorem we have

H^{j}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=H^{j}(X,K_{X}+\lceil-(K_{X}+D)+\epsilon D\rceil)=0

(3)

for all
j>0
.
For any irreducible component
D_{i}
of
D
,
-(K_{D_{i}}+D^{i})=-(K_{X}+D)|_{D_{i}}
is ample, so we also have

H^{j}(D_{i},{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{i}})=0

(4)

for
j>0
(note that the above argument for vanishing did not use
b_{2}=1
).
Since
b_{2}(X)=1
, we have
h^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})=1
. Let us consider the Atiyah extension of
{\mathcal{L}}

0\to\Omega_{X}^{1}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to 0.

Since
c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})\in H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})
is non-zero, the sequence is non-split. Hence the connecting homomorphism
H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\to H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})
is non-zero. Since
H^{1}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=0
(see (
3
)), this gives

H^{1}(X,{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{L}}})=0.

(5)

Let

0\to\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to 0

be the logarithmic Atiyah extension of
{\mathcal{L}}
. The short exact sequence

0\to\Omega_{X}^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)\longrightarrow\bigoplus{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{i}}\to 0

(6)

induces the short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{i}}\to 0.

The long exact cohomology sequence for this sequence and (
5
) show that

H^{1}(X,{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}})=0.

(7)

In the following we will also use the short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}})\longrightarrow{\mathbb{L}}\to 0.

(8)

Now we divide the proof into two cases depending on the extension (
2
).

Case 1.
If the extension (
2
) does not split then
s\leq r
and there exists a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r+1-s)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{L}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{X}(-\log D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{X}(-\log D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

The middle column of this diagram induces a short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r-s)}\to{\mathbb{L}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}\to 0.

Since the corresponding Ext group vanishes by (
7
), this sequence splits and
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r-s)}\oplus{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}
.
Since
{\mathop{{\rm Ext\,}}}^{1}({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=H^{1}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r-s)}\oplus{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}})=0
by (
3
) and (
7
),
sequence (
8
) splits. Therefore

{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r-s+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}.

By Proposition
3.9
there exists an isomorphism
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}})\simeq{\mathcal{L}}\otimes({\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}}))^{*}
.
So

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r-s+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}\simeq{\mathcal{L}}^{\oplus(r-s+1)}\oplus({\mathcal{L}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}).

By the Krull–Schmidt theorem, a decomposition of a vector bundle into a direct sum of indecomposable bundles is unique up to reordering of the factors (see
[
Ati56
]
). In particular,
{\mathcal{L}}^{\oplus(r-s+1)}
is a direct factor of

{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}
. But we have a short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\longrightarrow T_{X}(-\log D)\to 0.

So if
s<r+1
then we get a non-zero map
{\mathcal{L}}\to T_{X}(-\log D)
. Since
{\mathcal{L}}
is ample and
-(K_{X}+D)=(r+1)c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
, Theorem
1.1
shows that
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}
,
{\mathcal{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1)
and
D
is an arrangement of
s
general hyperplanes.
If
s=0
then
{\mathcal{B}}^{*}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}
.
If
s>0
then
T_{X}(-\log D)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}^{\oplus(s-1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}

by
[
DK93
, Proposition 2.10]
. In both cases we have

{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}^{\oplus(r+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}.

Case 2.
If the extension (
2
) splits then
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r-s+1)}\oplus T_{X}(-\log D)
. If the sequence (
8
) splits then

{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r-s+2)}\oplus T_{X}(-\log D).

So we have an isomorphism

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r-s+2)}\oplus T_{X}(-\log D)\simeq{\mathcal{L}}^{\oplus(r-s+2)}\oplus({\mathcal{L}}\otimes\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)).

Then
{\mathcal{L}}^{\oplus(r-s+2)}
is a direct factor of
T_{X}(-\log D)
. As above Theorem
1.1
implies that
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}
,
{\mathcal{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1)
and
D
is an arrangement of
s
general hyperplanes. But in this case
T_{X}(-\log D)
contains only
(r-s+1)
copies of
{\mathcal{L}}
, a contradiction.
It follows that the sequence (
8
) does not split.
However, by (
3
) we have

\text{Ext}^{1}({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{O}}_{X})=H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{L}}^{*})\simeq H^{1}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})^{\oplus(r-s+1)}\oplus H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D))=H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)).

By (
4
) the canonical map
H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})\to H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D))
, obtained from sequence (
6
), is surjective and the only non-zero class in
H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D))
(up to scalar) is equal to the image of
c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
. So
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus(r-s+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}.
As above this implies that if
s<r+1
then
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}
,
D
is an arrangement of
s
general hyperplanes and
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}^{\oplus(r+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}.
∎

Existence of semi-contact structure in case (2) is described in the following example:

Example 4.8
.

Let us first consider
X={\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1}
with an arrangement
D
of
(r+1)
hyperplanes
H_{1},...,H_{r+1}
in a general position.
Then
(X,D)
has a contact structure given by projection

T_{X}(-\log D)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus r}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)
onto one of the factors.
For every
0\leq s\leq r
, restricting to
H_{1}\cap...\cap H_{r+1-s}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}
and using Lemma
4.2
, we get a semi-contact structure on

{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}
with an arrangement of
s
hyperplanes in a general position.

Note that in the proof of Proposition
4.7
we did not use the Lie algebra structure on
{\mathbb{L}}
. But we use it to show the following slightly stronger form of the first assertion.

Lemma 4.9
.

If
(X,D)
is a semi-contact snc pair of rank
(2r+1)
then
r\leq\dim X
.

Proof.

Let
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
be a special contact Lie algebroid of rank
(2r+1)
on
(X,D)

with
{\mathcal{F}}=\ker(\theta:{\mathbb{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}})
. By definition we have an abelian extension

0\to{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{L}}\to T_{X}(-\log D)\to 0

of Lie algebroids.

We claim that the map
\gamma:{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathop{{\mathcal{H}}om}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
mapping
x\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}
to
the map
y\to\theta([x,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}})
is
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-linear.
Let us first note that for any
x\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
the map
{\mathbb{L}}\to{\mathbb{L}}
sending
y
to
[x,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}}
is
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-linear. Indeed, for any
x\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}
and
y\in{\mathbb{L}}

the Lie algebroid Leibniz rule gives

[x,fy]_{{\mathbb{L}}}=\alpha_{x}(f)y+f[x,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}}=f[x,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}}.

This implies that the map
y\to\theta([x,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}})
is also
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-linear and hence
\gamma
is well-defined.

Now we need to check that for any
f\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
and
x\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}

we have
\gamma(fx)=f\gamma(x)
.
For any
y\in{\mathbb{L}}
we have

\gamma(fx)(y)=\theta([fx,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}}).

Using the skew-symmetry of the Lie bracket and the standard Leibniz rule with respect to the anchor map
\alpha:{\mathbb{L}}\to T_{X}(-\log D)
, we can expand the bracket:

[fx,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}}=-[y,fx]_{{\mathbb{L}}}=-\left(f[y,x]_{{\mathbb{L}}}+\alpha_{y}(f)x\right)=f[x,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}}-\alpha_{y}(f)x.

Now, apply the
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-linear contact form
\theta
:

\theta([fx,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}})=f\theta([x,y]_{{\mathbb{L}}})-\alpha_{y}(f)\theta(x).

Since
x
lies in
{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}
, we have
\theta(x)=0
, which with the above implies
\gamma(fx)(y)=f\gamma(x)(y)
.

Now let us recall that by definition of a contact structure, the map
\hat{\theta}:{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathop{{\mathcal{H}}om}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{L}})
,
x\to\theta([x,\cdot]_{L})
is an isomorphism.
This implies that the map
\hat{\theta}|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}}
is injective. But this map is the composition of
\gamma
with the canonical projection

{\mathop{{\mathcal{H}}om}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})\to{\mathop{{\mathcal{H}}om}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{L}})
, so
\gamma
is also injective.
Since
[x,{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}]_{{\mathbb{L}}}=0
and
{\mathbb{L}}/{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\simeq T_{X}(-\log D)
, this implies that
the composition of
\gamma
with the canonical projection

{\mathop{{\mathcal{H}}om}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})\to{\mathop{{\mathcal{H}}om}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}(T_{X}(-\log D),{\mathcal{L}})
is also injective.
Therefore
{{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}}
has rank
\leq\dim X
.
But
{{\mathbb{L}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{F}}}
is the kernel of the map
\alpha\oplus\theta:{\mathbb{L}}\to T_{X}(-\log D)\oplus{\mathcal{L}}
, so it has rank
\geq(2r+1)-(\dim X+1)=2r-\dim X
. This shows that
r\leq\dim X
.
∎

4.3
Kodaira dimension of semi-contact snc pairs

In the following we write
\kappa({\mathcal{M}})
for the Iitaka dimension of
{\mathcal{M}}
(see
[
Laz04
, Definition 2.1.3]
).
The proof of the following lemma follows that of
[
Dru98
, Proposition 2]
.

Lemma 4.10
.

If
(X,D,{\mathcal{L}})
is a generically contact projective snc pair then
\kappa({\mathcal{L}}^{-1})=-\infty
.
In particular, if
(X,D)
is contact then
\kappa(K_{X}+D)=-\infty
.

Proof.

Assume that
\kappa({\mathcal{L}}^{-1})\geq 0
. By
[
Laz04
, Proposition 4.1.6]
there exists a smooth projective variety
\bar{X}
and a generically finite morphism
\pi:\bar{X}\to X
such that
h^{0}(\bar{X},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{-1})>0
, where
\bar{\mathcal{L}}=\pi^{*}{\mathcal{L}}
. Passing to a log resolution, we can also assume that
\bar{D}:=(\pi^{*}D)_{\sf red}
is a simple normal crossing divisor.
By assumption there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset
U\subset X
and
\omega\in H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\mathcal{L}})
such that
\omega|_{U}
is an
{\mathcal{L}}|_{U}
-valued contact form on
U
. There also exists a non-empty Zariski open subset
V\subset U\backslash{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D
such that
\bar{V}=\pi^{-1}(V)\to V
is finite étale. Taking any non-zero section
s\in H^{0}(\bar{X},\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{-1})
we can find a non-empty Zariski open subset
W\subset\bar{V}
such that
s
has no zeroes on
W
. Note that by the logarithmic ramification formula, we have a natural injective
{\mathcal{O}}_{\bar{X}}
-linear map
\pi^{*}\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)\hookrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{\bar{X}}(\log\bar{D})
.
Composing it with
\pi^{*}\omega
we obtain

\bar{\omega}:\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}\to\Omega^{1}_{\bar{X}}(\log\bar{D})
. Let us set
\eta:=\bar{\omega}(s)\in H^{0}(\bar{X},\Omega_{\bar{X}}^{1}(\log\bar{D})).

Since
\bar{\omega}|_{W}=\pi^{*}(\omega|_{V})|_{W}
gives an
\bar{\mathcal{L}}|_{W}
-valued contact form, we have
d(\eta|_{W})\neq 0
in

H^{0}(W,\Omega^{2}_{\bar{X}}|_{W})=H^{0}(W,\Omega^{2}_{\bar{X}}(\log\bar{D})|_{W})
. But by degeneracy of the logarithmic Hodge to de Rham spectral sequence on
(\bar{X},\bar{D})
, all global logarithmic
1
-forms on
(\bar{X},\bar{D})
are closed. Hence
d\eta=0\in H^{0}(\bar{X},\Omega^{2}_{\bar{X}}(\log\bar{D}))
. So
d(\eta|_{W})=d\eta|_{W}
also vanishes, a contradiction.
∎

Let us recall that a line bundle
{\mathcal{M}}
on a smooth projective variety
X
is
pseudoeffective
if and only if
c_{1}({\mathcal{M}})\in N^{1}(X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}
lies in the closure of the cone generated by classes of effective divisors.
The following theorem was proven by J.-P. Demailly in
[
Dem02
, Corollary 1]
in the non-logarithmic case and then generalized by F. Touzet in
[
Tou16
, Theorem 5 and Corollary 7.6]
to the logarithmic case.

Theorem 4.11
.

Let
(X,D)
be a projective snc pair and let
{\mathcal{L}}
be a line bundle on
X
. If
{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}
is pseudo-effective and
\omega\in H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)\otimes{\mathcal{L}})
is a logarithmic
1
-form with values in
{\mathcal{L}}
then
\omega\wedge d\omega=0
.

This theorem implies a much stronger form of Lemma
4.10
:

Corollary 4.12
.

Let
(X,D)
be a projective snc pair.
If
(X,D,{\mathcal{L}})
is generically contact then
{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}
is not pseudo-effective. In particular,

\kappa({\mathcal{L}}^{-1})=-\infty
. If
(X,D)
is a contact snc pair then
K_{X}+D
is not pseudoeffective. In particular,
X
is uniruled.

The following lemma deals with semi-contact, non-contact snc pairs.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
be a special generically contact Lie algebroid of rank
(2r+1)
on a projective snc pair
(X,D)
. If
\dim X<2r+1
then
H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{L}})\neq 0
.

Proof.

By assumption, we have a short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus s}\to{\mathbb{L}}\to T_{X}(-\log D)\to 0,

in which
s\geq 1
. If
H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{L}})=0
then the composition
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus s}\to{\mathbb{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}}
vanishes. So
{\mathbb{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}}
induces a generically surjective map
T_{X}(-\log D)\to{\mathcal{L}}
. Passing to the dual we see that the contact form

\omega:{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}\to\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}
factors through
{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}\to\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)
. But then

\omega\wedge(d_{{\mathbb{L}}}\omega)^{\wedge r}:{\mathcal{L}}^{-(r+1)}\to\det\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}

factors through
{\mathcal{L}}^{-(r+1)}\to\Omega_{X}^{2r+1}(\log D)=0
and we get a contradicton.
∎

Corollary 4.14
.

Let
(X,D)
be a projective snc pair.
If
(X,D,{\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
is semi-contact and
K_{X}+D
is pseudoeffective then
\dim X<{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}{\mathbb{L}}
and

{\mathcal{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
(in particular,
K_{X}+D\sim 0
).

Proof.

By Corollary
4.12
,
\dim X<{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}{\mathbb{L}}
. So Lemma
4.13
implies that

H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{L}})\neq 0
. Since the cone of pseudo-effective divisors is convex and
{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}
is pseudo-effective, the class of
{\mathcal{L}}
in
N^{1}(X)
vanishes. Since
{\mathcal{L}}
has a non-zero section this gives
{\mathcal{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
.
∎

The following example shows that semi-contact snc pairs with
K_{X}+D\sim 0
really occur.

Example 4.15
.

Let
(X,D)
be a projective snc pair of dimension
n
and assume that a semi-abelian variety
G
acts on
X
with
X\backslash{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D
as an open orbit. Since
G
is a commutative
k
-group, the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
of
G
is abelian. By assumption
\mathfrak{g}
acts on
X
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{g}
has a natural structure of Lie algebroid described in
[
BB93
, 1.2.2]
. The image of the anchor map
\alpha:{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{g}\to T_{X}
coincides with
T_{X}(-\log D)
(see, e.g.,
[
Win04
, p. 478]
). The Lie bracket
on
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{g}
is defined by requiring

[1\otimes\delta,1\otimes\delta^{\prime}]=1\otimes[\delta,\delta^{\prime}]=0

for
\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{g}
and extending it to
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{g}
by the Leibniz rule.

Let
\sigma
be a non-degenerate alternating form on a
2r
-dimensional
k
-vector space
V
and let
\mathfrak{h}=V\oplus k\cdot z
be a Lie
k
-algebra of dimension
(2r+1)
with the Lie bracket determined by

[v,w]=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\sigma(v,w)\cdot z&\hbox{if $v,w\in V$,}\\
0&\hbox{if $v\in V$ and $w=z$.}\\
\end{array}\right.

Assume that
r\leq n\leq 2r
and let
W\subset V
be an isotropic
k
-linear subspace of dimension
(2r-n)
.
Then
I=W\oplus k\cdot z\subset\mathfrak{h}
is an ideal and the quotient
\mathfrak{h}/I
is an abelian Lie algebra of dimension
n
, so it is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{g}
.
Let us fix a surjective map of Lie algebras
\mathfrak{h}\to\mathfrak{g}
.
Then
{\mathbb{L}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{h}
has a canonical structure of a Lie algebroid
for which the extension

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\otimes_{k}I\to{\mathbb{L}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{g}\simeq T_{X}(-\log D)\to 0

is abelian (one can again use
[
BB93
, 1.2.2]
). A contact structure on
{\mathbb{L}}
is defined by the map
{\mathbb{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
induced by the projection
\mathfrak{g}\to k\cdot z
. In this way for every
n/2\leq r\leq n
we obtain a rank
(2r+1)
semi-contact structure on
(X,D)
. Note that
the above extension is central only for
n=2r
.

The following example shows that Corollary
4.14
fails if
(X,D,{\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
is only generically semi-contact.

Example 4.16
.

Let
X=C_{1}\times C_{2}\times C_{3}
, where the
C_{i}
are smooth projective curves of genus at least
2
, and take
D=0
.
For
i=1,2,3
choose nonconstant rational functions on
C_{i}
, and let
x_{i}
be the induced rational functions on
X
.
Let us set

\eta=x_{1}\,dx_{2}+dx_{3}.

Then
d\eta=dx_{1}\wedge dx_{2}

and hence

\eta\wedge d\eta=dx_{1}\wedge dx_{2}\wedge dx_{3}\neq 0.

After choosing an effective divisor
E
containing all poles of
\eta
,
\eta
defines a global section of
\Omega_{X}^{1}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(E)
.
It therefore gives a generically
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(E)
-valued contact structure on
{\mathbb{L}}:=T_{X}
. But

K_{X}
is ample and
\dim X={\mathop{\rm rk\,}}{\mathbb{L}}
.

4.4
Lines on semi-contact snc pairs

Let us first note the following easy lemma:

Lemma 4.17
.

Let

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\to 0

be an exact sequence of vector bundles on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
. Then one of the following holds:

1.

the sequence does not split and
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}={\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}{\mathcal{E}}_{1}-1
, or

2.

the sequence splits and
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}={\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
.

Proof.

If the sequence splits then the assertion is clear. If it does not split then
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}{\mathcal{E}}>0
. In this case,
after dualizing the above sequence, the required equality follows from
[
KPSW00
, Lemma 2.7]
.
∎

By passing to the dual sequence and induction on
m
we get the following corollary:

Corollary 4.18
.

Let

0\to{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus m}\to 0

be an exact sequence of vector bundles on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
. Then

{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{*}\leq{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{*}

and equality holds if and only if the sequence splits.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
be a rank
(2r+1)
special contact Lie algebroid on a projective snc pair
(X,D)
. Let
f:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to C\subset X
be a normalization of a rational curve with
\deg f^{*}{\mathcal{L}}>0
.
Then we have
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)\leq r+1
. Moreover, if
C\cap{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D=\emptyset
then

{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}T_{X}\leq r
.

Proof.

Let us set
{\mathcal{B}}={\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}({\mathcal{L}})
and
consider the
{\mathbb{L}}
-Atiyah extension of
{\mathcal{L}}

0\to\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to 0.

By Proposition
3.9
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{L}})={\mathcal{B}}^{*}
carries an
{\mathcal{L}}
-valued symplectic structure and hence we have an isomorphism
{\mathcal{B}}\simeq{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}^{*}
. So for some integers
a_{1}\geq...\geq a_{r+1}\geq 0
we have

f^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r+1}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(a_{i})\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-a_{i}-d)),

where
d=\deg f^{*}{\mathcal{L}}>0
.
In particular,
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}({\mathcal{B}}^{*})=r+1
.
If the sequence

0\to f^{*}\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}\to f^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\to f^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}\to 0

does not split then by Lemma
4.17
we have
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}{\mathbb{L}}=r
.
If the above sequence splits then
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}{\mathbb{L}}=r+1
.

We also have a short exact sequence

0\to f^{*}\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)\to f^{*}\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus(2r+1-\dim X)}\to 0

obtained by pulling back the dual to the sequence defining a special contact Lie algebroid.
So using Corollary
4.18
we get
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)\leq r+1
with equality if and only if both the above sequences split.

Now assume that
C\cap{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D=\emptyset
so that
f^{*}\Omega_{X}^{1}\simeq f^{*}\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)
. By construction of the Atiyah extensions, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{*}\Omega_{X}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{L}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{*}\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{\oplus m}}

If
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}T_{X}={\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}T_{X}(-\log D)=r+1
then we have an induced splitting

f^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{L}}})\to f^{*}{\mathcal{B}}\to f^{*}\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}\to f^{*}\Omega_{X}^{1}

of the first row in the above diagram. But
\deg f^{*}{\mathcal{L}}\neq 0
, so the commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{*}\Omega_{X}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{*}({\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{L}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}_{f^{*}{\mathcal{L}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

shows that this sequence does not split. Therefore
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}T_{X}\leq r
.
∎

Lemma 4.20
.

Let
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
be a rank
(2r+1)
special contact Lie algebroid on a projective snc pair
(X,D)
. Assume that
K_{X}+D
is not nef and let
\varphi
be a good log contraction of
(X,D)
.
Then either
(X,D,{\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1},0,T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1}}(2))
or there exists a
\varphi
-exceptional rational curve
C
with
{\mathcal{L}}\cdot C=1
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we can assume that
\varphi
is elementary and it contracts some
(K_{X}+D)
-extremal ray
R
. If
(X,D)\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1},0)
then
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})\simeq(T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1}}(2))
. Otherwise,
Lemma
1.6
implies that
l_{D}(\varphi)\leq 2r+1
. Since
-(K_{X}+D)=(r+1)c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
,
l_{D}(\varphi)
is divisible by
r+1
. Therefore
l_{D}(\varphi)=r+1
and if
C
is an extremal rational curve with
[C]\in R
then
{\mathcal{L}}\cdot C=1
.
∎

If
(X,D)
is a contact projective snc pair then Corollary
4.12
implies that
K_{X}+D
is not nef. So we have the following corollary.

Corollary 4.21
.

If
(X,D,{\mathcal{L}})
is a contact projective snc pair and
(X,D,{\mathcal{L}})\not\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1},0,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1}}(2))
then
K_{X}+D
is not nef and for every good log contraction
\varphi
there exists an extremal rational curve
C
with
{\mathcal{L}}\cdot C=1
.

5
Log contractions of semi-contact snc pairs

This section contains proof of a generalization of Theorem
0.1
.
By Corollary
1.8
good log contractions of semi-contact varieties induce good log contractions on the strata of stratification induced by
D
. This allows us to reduce to studying good log contractions, whose exceptional locus is not contained in the boundary. We call such contractions
very good
.

In this section we use results of Section
2
to describe the general fiber and we prove that good log contractions of semi-contact varieties are equidimensional. This allows us to conclude that our contractions are projective bundles (we also need to prove this for every stratum of the stratification induced by the boundary divisor). The main result of the section is Theorem
5.14
. Its proof requires further study of the geometry using various Atiyah extensions.

From now on in this section we fix the following notation. Let
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
be a special contact Lie algebroid of rank
(2r+1)
on a projective snc pair
(Z,D^{Z})
. Let us write
n=\dim Z
and
U_{Z}=Z\backslash{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D^{Z}
.
Assume that
(K_{Z}+D^{Z})
is not nef so that
(Z,D^{Z})
admits a good log contraction
\varphi_{Z}:Z\to S
. Let
F
be the
(K_{Z}+D^{Z})
-extremal face corresponding to
\varphi_{Z}
. Till the end of the section we assume that the exceptional locus
{\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi_{Z})
is not contained in the support of
D^{Z}
, i.e., that
\varphi_{Z}
is very good. We also assume that
(Z,D^{Z},{\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})\not\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1},0,T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1}},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2r+1}}(2))
.

5.1
General structure of log contractions

Let
C
be an extremal rational curve with
[C]\in F
and let
f:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to C
be the normalization of
C
. We can choose
C
so that
C\cap U_{Z}\neq\emptyset
. By Proposition
2.1
we can also assume that
f^{*}D^{Z}
is supported in at most one point so that
f^{-1}(U_{Z})={\mathbb{P}}^{1}
or
f^{-1}(U_{Z})\simeq{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.

If
H_{F}
is a supporting divisor of
F
then for large
m
the divisor
mH_{F}+c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
is ample and by Lemma
4.20
the degree of
C
with respect to this divisor is
1
. In particular, any closed subset of
the connected component of
{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},Z)
containing
[f]
is unsplit (a reducible degeneration would give a curve whose class has
(mH_{F}+c_{1}({\mathcal{L}}))
-degree strictly smaller than
1
).

Let
V
be an irreducible component of
{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},Z)
, which contains
[f]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

If
f^{-1}(U_{Z})={\mathbb{P}}^{1}
then
{\rm locus}(V)=Z
and
\dim{\rm locus}(V,x)=r
for any
x\in U_{Z}
. Moreover, we have
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}T_{Z}=r
.

Proof.

Since the family
V
is unsplit, Proposition
2.3
gives

\dim{\rm locus}(V)+\dim{\rm locus}(V,x)+1\geq\dim V\geq-K_{Z}\cdot C+\dim Z\geq-(K_{Z}+D^{Z})\cdot C+\dim Z=r+1+n

But
\dim{\rm locus}(V)\leq n
, so
\dim{\rm locus}(V,x)\geq r
for all
x\in Z
.
Lemma
4.19
implies that

r\leq\dim{\rm locus}(V,x)\leq{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}f^{*}T_{Z}\leq r.

So
\dim{\rm locus}(V,x)=r
for all
x\in U_{Z}
. This implies that
\dim{\rm locus}(V)=n
and hence

{\rm locus}(V)=Z
.
∎

Let
W
be an irreducible component of
{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},Z;f^{*}D^{Z}\subset D^{Z})
containing
[f]
.

Lemma 5.2
.

If
f^{-1}(U_{Z})\simeq{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
then
{\rm locus}(W)=Z
and
\dim{\rm locus}_{U_{Z}}(W,x)=r+1
for any
x\in U_{Z}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.4
we have

\dim{\rm locus}_{U_{Z}}(W)+\dim{\rm locus}_{U_{Z}}(W,x)\geq\dim W\geq-(K_{Z}+D^{Z})\cdot C+\dim Z=r+1+n.

By Lemma
4.19
for any
g:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to X
such that
[g]\in W
, we have
{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}g^{*}T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})\leq r+1
. So by Proposition
2.5
we have

\dim{\rm locus}_{U_{Z}}(W,x)\leq r+1.
Since
\dim{\rm locus}_{U_{Z}}(W)\leq n
, the above inequality
implies that
\dim{\rm locus}_{U_{Z}}(W)=n
and
\dim{\rm locus}_{U_{Z}}(W,x)=r+1
. ∎

Remark 5.3
.

Note that if
f^{-1}(U_{Z})={\mathbb{P}}^{1}
then
f^{*}D^{Z}=0
and
W=V
. So it is always sufficient to consider
W
. We keep the above notation to show differences and similarities to the usual non-logarithmic case (and anyway we need to consider two cases).

The following corollary is an analogue of
[
KPSW00
, Lemma 2.10]
.

Corollary 5.4
.

The contraction
\varphi_{Z}:Z\to S
is of fiber type, i.e.,
\dim S<\dim Z
.

Proof.

Since
C
is an extremal rational curve, every element of
V
gives a curve contracted by
\varphi_{Z}
.
But Lemmas
5.1
and
5.2
imply that
{\rm locus}(V)=Z
, so
\varphi_{Z}
cannot be birational. ∎

The following proposition is an analogue of
[
KPSW00
, Proposition 2.11]
in our setting. The strategy of its proof is similar but we have more cases than just one.

Proposition 5.5
.

Assume that with
\dim S>0
. Then there exists an open subset
U\subset S
such that for some
0\leq s\leq r
and for any closed point
y\in U
we have
(Z_{y},D_{y}^{Z})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r+s},H_{1}+...+H_{s})
, where
H_{1},...,H_{s}
are hyperplanes in a general position. Moreover, we have
{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z_{y}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1)
.

Proof.

By generic smoothness in characteristic zero, there exists an open subset
U\subset S
such that
\varphi|_{Z_{U}}:Z_{U}\to U
is smooth and

D_{U}^{Z}\subset Z_{U}
is a divisor with strict normal crossings relative to
U
. In particular, for every
y\in U
we have a short exact sequence

0\to T_{Z_{y}}(-\log D^{Z}_{y})\to T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})|_{Z_{y}}\to\varphi^{*}T_{S}|_{Z_{y}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{y}}^{\dim S}\to 0.

Let us consider the map
\alpha:\Omega_{Z}^{1}(\log D^{Z})\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\to T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})
given by composition

\Omega_{Z}^{1}(\log D^{Z})\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\hookrightarrow\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}^{*}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\simeq}{\mathcal{F}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{L}}\twoheadrightarrow T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z}).

This yields the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{T_{Z_{y}}(-\log D_{y}^{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\varphi^{*}\Omega_{S}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})|_{Z_{y}}\simeq({\mathcal{L}}|_{Z_{y}})^{\oplus\dim S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{0}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{(\Omega_{Z}^{1}(\log D^{Z})\otimes{\mathcal{L}})|_{Z_{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha|_{Z_{y}}}
\textstyle{T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})|_{Z_{y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{y}}^{\dim S}}

The lower arrow is zero because
\dim Z_{y}>0
by Corollary
5.4
and
{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z_{y}}
is ample as
-(K_{Z}+D^{Z})
is
\varphi|_{Z}
-ample.
This implies existence of
\beta
.
The short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}^{\oplus(2r+1-n)}\to{\mathbb{L}}\to T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})\to 0

shows that the kernel of
\delta:({\mathcal{F}}^{*}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})|_{Z_{y}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{F}}|_{Z_{y}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{L}}|_{Z_{y}}\twoheadrightarrow T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})|_{Z_{y}}
is contained in
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{y}}^{\oplus(2r+1-n)}
. On the other hand, the short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{y}}\to(\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})|_{Z_{y}}\to({\mathcal{F}}^{*}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})|_{Z_{y}}\to 0

shows that the kernel of
\alpha|_{Z_{y}}
is contained in an extension of
\ker\delta
by
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{y}}
. So
\ker\alpha|_{Z_{y}}
is also contained in an extension of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{Z_{y}}}^{\oplus(2r+1-n)}
by
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z_{y}}
.
Since
\gamma
is injective this implies that the map
\beta
is non-zero. So there exists a non-zero map
{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z_{y}}\to T_{Z_{y}}(-\log D_{y}^{Z})
. By Theorem
1.1
we see that
for some
m
we have
Z_{y}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{m}
and
{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z_{y}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{m}}(1)
. Moreover,
D_{y}^{Z}

is a sum of
0\leq s\leq m
hyperplanes in a general position.
Let
C\subset Z_{y}
be a line. Then

r+1=-(K_{X}+D)\cdot C=-(K_{Z_{y}}+D_{y}^{Z})\cdot C_{y}=m+1-s,

so
m=r+s
. ∎

Remark 5.6
.

If
{\mathbb{L}}=T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})
and
s=0
, i.e.,
(Z_{y},D_{y}^{Z})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r},0)
, then the composition

T_{Z_{y}}=T_{Z_{y}}(-\log D_{y}^{Z})\to T_{Z}(-\log D^{Z})|_{Z_{y}}\to{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z_{y}}

vanishes and
T_{Z_{y}}\subset{\mathcal{F}}|_{Z_{y}}
. Indeed, since
{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}(T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1))=H^{0}({\mathbb{P}}^{r},\Omega^{1}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1))=0
, there are no non-zero maps
T_{Z_{y}}\to{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z_{y}}
. This gives a quick proof of the last part of
[
KPSW00
, Proposition 2.11]
.

Remark 5.7
.

Proposition
5.5
shows that
a posteriori a general fiber
(Z_{y},D_{y}^{Z})
of
\varphi_{Z}
is semi-contact with
b_{2}(Z_{y})=1
(see Proposition
4.7
and Example
4.8
).

Let
D^{Z}=\sum_{i\in I}D_{i}^{Z}
be the decomposition of
D^{Z}
into irreducible components. For a subset
J\subset I

and a connected component
Z^{\prime}
of
D_{J}^{Z}=\bigcap_{i\in J}D_{i}^{Z}
we write
D^{Z^{\prime}}
for the restriction of the divisor

\sum_{i\in I\backslash J}D_{i}^{Z}
to
Z^{\prime}
. If
i\in I\backslash J
then we also write

D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i}
for the restriction of
D_{i}^{Z}
to
Z^{\prime}
. We also set
U_{Z^{\prime}}:=Z^{\prime}\backslash{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D^{Z^{\prime}}
.

Assume that
\dim S>0
. Let
I^{h}\subset I
be the subset corresponding to the components dominating
S
and let

I^{v}:=I\backslash I^{h}
be its complement. Then we set
D^{h}:=\sum_{i\in I^{h}}D_{i}^{Z}
and
D^{v}:=\sum_{i\in I^{v}}D_{i}^{Z}
.
In the following
D^{h}
is called the
horizontal part
of
D^{Z}
and
D^{v}
is called the
vertical part
of
D^{Z}
.

By Proposition
5.5
we have
0\leq s=|I^{h}|\leq r
.
Let
U_{S}
be the complement of the image of
D^{v}
in
S
.
For a subset
J\subset I^{h}
, we set
U_{J}^{\prime}=D^{h}_{J}\cap\varphi_{Z}^{-1}(U_{S})
(note that
D^{h}_{J}
is irreducible). Clearly,
\varphi_{Z}(D^{h}_{J})=S
and
\varphi_{Z}|_{U_{J}^{\prime}}
is a projective morphism with a general fiber isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s-|J|}
. We need the following result on the dimension of its fibers.

Proposition 5.8
.

If
\dim S>0
then for any subset
J\subset I^{h}
(possibly empty), all fibers of

\varphi_{Z}|_{U_{J}^{\prime}}
have dimension
r+|I^{h}\backslash J|
.

Proof.

Since
-(K_{Z}+D^{Z})=(r+1)c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
is
\varphi_{Z}
-ample, for sufficiently ample line bundle
{\mathcal{A}}
on
T
the line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{L}}\otimes\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{A}}
is ample. The proof is by induction on the number of elements in
I^{h}\backslash J
.

Step 1.
Assume that
|I^{h}\backslash J|=0
, i.e.,
J=I^{h}
.

Let us consider
Z^{\prime}=D^{h}_{I^{h}}
(if
I^{h}=\emptyset
then we set
Z^{\prime}=Z
). In this case Proposition
5.5
implies that for general
y\in S
we have
(Z_{y}^{\prime},D_{y}^{Z^{\prime}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r},0)
and
{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z^{\prime}_{y}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1)
.
Let us consider the Chow variety
\mathop{\rm Chow\,}_{r,1}(Z^{\prime})
of
r
-dimensional cycles on
Z^{\prime}
of degree
1
with respect to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
and let
Q
be an irreducible component of
\mathop{\rm Chow\,}_{r,1}(Z^{\prime})
containing
Z^{\prime}_{y}
for general
y\in S
. Since
N_{Z^{\prime}_{y}/Z^{\prime}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}^{\dim S}
, we have
h^{1}(N_{Z^{\prime}_{y}/Z^{\prime}})=0

and
\dim Q=h^{0}(N_{Z^{\prime}_{y}/Z^{\prime}})=\dim S
. So
Q
is projective of dimension
\dim S
, and there exists a universal family of cycles
\tau:{\mathcal{U}}\to Q
with the evaluation map
{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to Z^{\prime}
.
Note that the canonical rational map
\psi:S\dashrightarrow Q
, mapping a general point
y\in S
to the cycle class

[Z^{\prime}_{y}]\in Q
, is dominant and birational. So
{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}
is also dominant and birational, and either it is an isomorphism or it has a positive dimensional fiber. In the first case,
(\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\to S)\simeq(\tau:{\mathcal{U}}\to Q)
, so
\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime}}
is equidimensional. In the second case, there exists
x_{0}\in Z^{\prime}
such that
\dim({\mathop{\rm ev\,}}^{-1}(x_{0}))>0
. Then by the defining property of
\mathop{\rm Chow\,}_{r,1}(Z^{\prime})
,
\dim({\mathop{\rm ev\,}}(\tau^{-1}(\tau({\mathop{\rm ev\,}}^{-1}(x_{0})))))>r
.

Let
V_{{\mathcal{U}}}\subset{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},{\mathcal{U}})
be an irreducible component containing some
f:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to{\mathcal{U}}
for which the image is a line in a general fiber
Z^{\prime}_{y}=\tau^{-1}([Z^{\prime}_{y}])\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
and
f
is an isomorphism onto its image. For
q\in Q
the fiber
\tau^{-1}(q)
represents an
r
-dimensional cycle of degree
1
with respect to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
. Since
{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}|_{\tau^{-1}(q)}
is finite,
({\mathop{\rm ev\,}})^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
is ample on every fiber of
\tau
and hence it is
\tau
-ample. Therefore
V_{{\mathcal{U}}}
parametrizes birational maps to curves of degree
1
with respect to some ample line bundle and hence the family
V_{{\mathcal{U}}}
is unsplit. Moreover, the natural map
\tilde{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}:{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},{\mathcal{U}})\to{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},Z^{\prime})
maps
V_{{\mathcal{U}}}
into an irreducible component
V
of
{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},Z^{\prime})
containing lines in a general fiber
Z^{\prime}_{y}
of
\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime}}
.
Since
(\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\to S)
and
(\tau:{\mathcal{U}}\to Q)
are isomorphic over dense open subsets where
\psi
is an isomorphism, we have
{\rm locus}(V_{{\mathcal{U}}},u)=\tau^{-1}(\tau(u))\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
for general
u\in{\mathcal{U}}
.
Since
V_{{\mathcal{U}}}
is unsplit, the equality
{\rm locus}(V_{{\mathcal{U}}},u)=\tau^{-1}(\tau(u))
holds for all
u\in{\mathcal{U}}
. This implies that
{\rm locus}(V,x_{0})\supset{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}(\tau^{-1}(\tau({\mathop{\rm ev\,}}^{-1}(x_{0}))))
has dimension
\geq(r+1)
.

Let us assume that
x_{0}\in U_{Z^{\prime}}
. If
g:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to Z^{\prime}
is a map from the family
V
and
x_{0}\in C:=g({\mathbb{P}}^{1})
then
C\subset U_{Z^{\prime}}
as
C
is not contained in the support of
D^{Z^{\prime}}
and
D^{Z^{\prime}}\cdot C=0
. But then Lemma
5.1
implies that
\dim{\rm locus}(V,x_{0})=r
, a contradiction. It follows that

\varphi_{Z}(x_{0})\not\in U_{S}
and all fibers of
\varphi_{Z}|_{U_{I^{h}}^{\prime}}:U_{I^{h}}^{\prime}\to U_{S}
have dimension
r
.

Step 2.
Assume that
J\subsetneq I^{h}
and the assertion holds for all subsets properly containing
J
.

Let us set
m=|I^{h}\backslash J|
and consider
Z^{\prime}=D^{h}_{J}
. By Proposition
5.5
for general
y\in S
we have
(Z_{y},D_{y}^{Z})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r+s},H_{1}+...+H_{s})
and
{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z_{y}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}}(1)
. This implies that
for general
y\in S
we have
(Z_{y}^{\prime},D_{y}^{Z^{\prime}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r+m},H_{1}+...+H_{m})
and
{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z_{y}^{\prime}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+m}}(1)
. Let us consider the Chow variety
\mathop{\rm Chow\,}_{r+m,1}(Z^{\prime})
of
(r+m)
-dimensional cycles on
Z^{\prime}
of degree
1
with respect to
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
. Let
Q
be an irreducible component of
\mathop{\rm Chow\,}_{r+m,1}(Z^{\prime})
containing general fibers
Z^{\prime}_{y}
of
\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime}}
. As in Step 1 this component is projective of dimension equal to the dimension of
S
and there exists a universal family of pairs of cycles
\tau:{\mathcal{U}}\to Q
with the evaluation map
{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to Z^{\prime}
. The map
{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}
is birational and either it is an isomorphism or it has a positive dimensional fiber. In the first case,
(\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\to S)\simeq(\tau:{\mathcal{U}}\to Q)
, so
\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime}}
is equidimensional.
In the second case, there exists
x_{0}\in Z^{\prime}
such that
\dim({\mathop{\rm ev\,}}^{-1}(x_{0}))>0
. Then by the defining property of
\mathop{\rm Chow\,}_{r+m,1}(Z^{\prime})
,
\dim({\mathop{\rm ev\,}}(\tau^{-1}(\tau({\mathop{\rm ev\,}}^{-1}(x_{0})))))>r+m
.

Let us choose some
f:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to{\mathcal{U}}
such that
f
is an isomorphism onto a line in a general fiber
Z^{\prime}_{y}=\tau^{-1}([Z^{\prime}_{y}])\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r+m}
, not contained in the support of
D^{Z^{\prime}}_{y}
. By
\tilde{f}
we denote the composition of
f
with
{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}
. Let us fix some
i_{0}\in I^{h}\backslash J
and let us set
D_{{\mathcal{U}}}:={\mathop{\rm ev\,}}^{*}D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}}
.
Let
W_{{\mathcal{U}}}\subset{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},{\mathcal{U}};f^{*}D_{{\mathcal{U}}}\subset D_{{\mathcal{U}}})
be an irreducible component containing
[f]
. the same arguments as in Step 1 show the family
W_{{\mathcal{U}}}
is unsplit (since it parametrizes birational morphisms to curves of degree
1
with respect to some ample line bundle) and the natural map
\tilde{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}:{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},{\mathcal{U}};f^{*}D_{{\mathcal{U}}}\subset D_{{\mathcal{U}}})\to{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},{Z^{\prime}};\tilde{f}^{*}D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}}\subset D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}})
maps
W_{{\mathcal{U}}}
into an irreducible component
W
of
{\mathop{{\rm Hom}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},{Z^{\prime}};\tilde{f}^{*}D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}}\subset D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}})
containing
[\tilde{f}]
.
For general
u\in{\mathcal{U}}
,
{\rm locus}(W_{{\mathcal{U}}},u)=\tau^{-1}(\tau(u))\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r+m}
. Since
W_{{\mathcal{U}}}
is unsplit, the equality
{\rm locus}(W_{{\mathcal{U}}},u)=\tau^{-1}(\tau(u))
holds for all
u\in{\mathcal{U}}
. This implies that
{\rm locus}(W,x_{0})\supset{\mathop{\rm ev\,}}(\tau^{-1}(\tau({\mathop{\rm ev\,}}^{-1}(x_{0}))))
, so

\dim{\rm locus}(W,x_{0})\geq(r+m+1).

(9)

By Lemma
4.6
,
(Z^{\prime},D^{Z^{\prime}})
is semi-contact of rank
(2r+1)
.
So by Lemma
4.19
for any
g:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to{Z^{\prime}}
with
[g]\in W
we have

{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}g^{*}T_{Z^{\prime}}(-\log D^{Z^{\prime}})\leq r+1.

If
g({\mathbb{P}}^{1})\cap{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D^{v}=\emptyset
then pulling back the short exact sequence

0\to T_{Z^{\prime}}(-\log(\sum_{i\in I^{h}\backslash J}D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i}))\longrightarrow T_{Z^{\prime}}(-\log D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i\in I^{h}\backslash(J\cup\{i_{0}\})}{\mathcal{O}}_{D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i}}(D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i})\to 0

by
g
we get an exact sequence

g^{*}T_{Z^{\prime}}(-\log D^{Z^{\prime}})\longrightarrow g^{*}T_{Z^{\prime}}(-\log D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(1)^{\oplus(m-1)}\to 0.

Then

{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}^{+}g^{*}T_{Z^{\prime}}(-\log D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}})\leq(r+1)+(m-1)=r+m.

Proposition
2.5
implies that
if
x_{0}\not\in D^{{Z^{\prime}}}_{i_{0}}\cup{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D^{h}
then
\dim{\rm locus}(W,x_{0})\leq r+m
,
a contradiction. If
x_{0}\in D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}}\backslash{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D^{h}
then by (
9
)

\dim{\rm locus}(W,x_{0})\cap D^{Z^{\prime}}_{i_{0}}\geq\dim{\rm locus}(W,x_{0})-1\geq r+m.

But
{\rm locus}(W,x_{0})
is contained in a fiber of
\varphi_{Z}
as it is covered by extremal curves passing through one point. So
{\rm locus}(W,x_{0})\cap D^{h}
is contained in
(\varphi_{Z}|_{{Z^{\prime}}\cap D^{h}_{i_{0}}})^{-1}(\varphi_{Z}(x_{0}))
, which
by the induction assumption has dimension
(r+m-1)
, a contradiction.
It follows that

\varphi_{Z}(x_{0})\not\in U_{S}
and all fibers of

\varphi_{Z}|_{U_{J}^{\prime}}
have dimension
(r+m)
.
∎

5.2
Classification of good elementary log contractions

The following proposition is an analogue of the first part of
[
KPSW00
, Theorem 2.12]
. As in
[
KPSW00
]
the result depends on Fujita’s theorem
[
Fuj87
, Lemma 2.12]
, but the proof is much more involved as we need to analyse the stratification of
Z
induced by the components of
D^{Z}
. We keep the notation from the previous subsection.

Proposition 5.9
.

Assume that
\varphi_{Z}
is elementary and
\dim S>0
. Then

S
is smooth and for any subset
J\subset I^{h}
(possibly empty),
\varphi_{Z}|_{D^{h}_{J}}:D^{h}_{J}\to S
is a projective bundle. Moreover, there exists a simple normal crossing divisor
B^{S}
on
S
such that irreducible components of
D^{v}
are preimages of irreducible components of
B^{S}
.

Proof.

Since
\varphi_{Z}
is elementary, all extremal rational curves contracted by
\varphi_{Z}
are proportional.
By Proposition
5.5
, for general
y\in S
we have
(Z_{y},D_{y}^{Z})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r+s},H_{1}+...+H_{s})
, so if
C
is an extremal rational curve then in
N_{1}(Z/S)
the class of
C
coincides with the class of a line in
Z_{y}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}
.
Therefore
C\cdot D_{i}^{Z}=1
for
i\in I^{h}
and
C\cdot D_{i}^{Z}=0
for all
i\in I^{v}
.
In particular, curves contracted by
\varphi_{Z}
do not properly intersect any irreducible component of
D^{v}
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
K\subset I^{v}
and let
W
be a connected component of
D_{K}^{Z}
.
Then
W
is saturated with respect to
\varphi_{Z}
, that is,

\varphi_{Z}^{-1}\bigl(\varphi_{Z}(W)\bigr)=W

set-theoretically.
The same assertions hold after restriction to a stratum. More precisely,
let
Z^{\prime}=D_{J}^{h}
, let
C\subset Z^{\prime}
be an extremal rational curve
contracted by
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}:=\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\to S
, let

K=\{i\in I^{v}:C\subset D_{i}^{Z}\}
,
and let
Z^{\prime\prime}
be the connected component of
D_{K}^{Z^{\prime}}
containing
C
. Then

1.

\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
is a good log contraction of
(Z^{\prime},D^{Z^{\prime}})
,

2.

S^{\prime\prime}:=\varphi_{Z}(Z^{\prime\prime})
is normal and
\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}:=\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}}:Z^{\prime\prime}\to S^{\prime\prime}
is a good log contraction of
(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})
,

3.

for every
i\in I^{v}\backslash K
, every
nonempty component of
D_{i}^{Z}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
is vertical with respect to

\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
,

4.

if

B_{i}^{S}:=\varphi_{Z}(D_{i}^{Z})
, then
S^{\prime\prime}\not\subset B_{i}^{S}
and

\operatorname{Supp}\bigl(D_{i}^{Z}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}}\bigr)=\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}\bigl(S^{\prime\prime}\cap B_{i}^{S}\bigr)

set-theoretically.

Proof.

Let
F
be a fiber of

\varphi_{Z}
meeting
W
. We claim that
F\subset D_{i}^{Z}
for every

i\in K
. Otherwise, since
F
is connected and projective, we could
find an irreducible curve in
F
properly intersecting
D_{i}^{Z}
. Since this is
impossible we have
F\subset D_{K}^{Z}
. Since

F
is connected and meets the connected component
W
, we have

F\subset W
. It follows that

\varphi_{Z}^{-1}\bigl(\varphi_{Z}(W)\bigr)=W

set-theoretically.

Points (1) and (2) follow from Corollary
1.8
. The same argument as above applies to the contraction

\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\to S
. Let
i\in I^{v}\backslash K
, the saturation
statement gives

\operatorname{Supp}\bigl(D_{i}^{Z}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}}\bigr)=Z^{\prime\prime}\cap\varphi_{Z}^{-1}(B_{i}^{S})=\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}(S^{\prime\prime}\cap B_{i}^{S}).

If
S^{\prime\prime}\subset B_{i}^{S}
, then the saturation of
D_{i}^{Z}
would imply

Z^{\prime\prime}\subset D_{i}^{Z}
, and hence
C\subset D_{i}^{Z}
, contradicting

i\notin K
. Therefore
S^{\prime\prime}\cap B_{i}^{S}
is a proper closed subset of

S^{\prime\prime}
, and every component of
D_{i}^{Z}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
is vertical with respect
to
\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
.
∎

As in the proof of Proposition
5.8
the proof is by induction on the number of elements in
I^{h}\backslash J
.

Step 1.
Let us first consider the case
J=I^{h}
. Let us set

Z^{\prime}:=D^{h}_{I^{h}}
. By
Proposition
5.5
, for general
y\in S
we
have
(Z^{\prime}_{y},D_{y}^{Z^{\prime}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r},0)
and

{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z^{\prime}_{y}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1)
.
Let
F
be an arbitrary fiber of
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
, taken with the reduced structure. Since the general
fiber has dimension
r
, we have

\dim F\geq r
. Choose a projective curve
C^{\prime}
contained in an
irreducible component of
F
of maximal dimension. By Proposition

1.5
, we can find an extremal rational curve

C
contracted by
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
, lying in the same fiber and in the
same stratum of the stratification of
Z^{\prime}
induced by the components
of
D^{Z^{\prime}}
. Let
f:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to C
be the normalization of
C
. Let
K

be the set of all
j\in I^{v}
for which
C\subset D_{j}^{Z}
, and let

Z^{\prime\prime}
be the connected component of
D_{K}^{Z^{\prime}}
containing
C
.

By Lemma
5.10
,
S^{\prime\prime}:=\varphi_{Z}(Z^{\prime\prime})
is normal
and
\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}:=\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}}:Z^{\prime\prime}\to S^{\prime\prime}
is a good log
contraction of
(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})
. Moreover, all components of

D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}
are vertical with respect to
\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
. By Lemma

4.6
,
(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})
is
semi-contact of rank
2r+1
, and hence Corollary
5.4

gives
\dim S^{\prime\prime}<\dim Z^{\prime\prime}
.

By the definition of
K
, the curve
C
is not contained in

\operatorname{Supp}D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}
. Lemma
5.10
therefore
implies that
C\subset U_{Z^{\prime\prime}}:=\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}(U_{S^{\prime\prime}})
. If

\dim S^{\prime\prime}>0
, Proposition
5.8
shows that
the fiber of
\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
containing
C
has dimension
r
. On the
other hand, Lemma
5.10
shows that the fiber
F

is contained in
Z^{\prime\prime}
. Consequently,
F
is the fiber of

\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
containing
C
, and hence
\dim F=r
.

Lemma 5.11
.

If
\dim S^{\prime\prime}=0
then
(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r},0)
.

Proof.

Vanishing
D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}=0
is clear. Otherwise,
Lemma
5.10
gives

Z^{\prime\prime}=\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}(\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}(D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}))=D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}
, which is impossible because
D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}
is a divisor in
Z^{\prime\prime}
.

By construction
-K_{Z^{\prime\prime}}=(r+1)c_{1}({\mathcal{L}}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}})
is ample, so
Z^{\prime\prime}
is Fano.
If
b_{2}(Z^{\prime\prime})>1
then by
[
Wiś90
, Theorem B]

Z^{\prime\prime}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
.
Note that

\dim Z^{\prime\prime}\leq\dim Z^{\prime}-1\leq\dim Z-1\leq 2r.

Since
\dim Z^{\prime\prime}=2r
, we have
Z^{\prime}=Z
and
Z^{\prime\prime}
is a divisor in
Z
.
By Lemma
5.10
,
Z^{\prime\prime}
is the geometric fiber of

\varphi_{Z}
(taken with the reduced structure) over the point
\varphi_{Z}(Z^{\prime\prime})
. Every curve contained in

Z^{\prime\prime}
is contracted by
\varphi_{Z}
, so the conormal line bundle of
Z^{\prime\prime}
has degree zero
on both rulings of
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
. Hence

N^{*}_{Z^{\prime\prime}/Z}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}.

Since
H^{1}(Z^{\prime\prime},{\mathcal{O}}_{Z^{\prime\prime}})=0
, the assumptions of Castelnuovo’s
criterion
[
AW97
, Proposition 3.8]
are
satisfied. Therefore
\dim S=h^{0}(Z^{\prime\prime},N^{*}_{Z^{\prime\prime}/Z})=1
.
On the other hand, the general fiber of
\varphi_{Z}
has dimension
r
,
so
\dim S=\dim Z-r=r+1
, a contradiction.

It follows that
b_{2}(Z^{\prime\prime})=1
. Since
\dim Z^{\prime\prime}\leq 2r
and
D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}=0
, Proposition
4.7
implies that
Z^{\prime\prime}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
.
∎

If
\dim S^{\prime\prime}=0
then Lemma
5.10
shows that
Z^{\prime\prime}=F
. So the above lemma implies that also in this case
\dim F=r
. Thus every fiber of
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
has dimension
r
. Fujita’s theorem

[
Fuj87
, Lemma 2.12]
now implies that
S
is smooth and

\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
is a projective bundle.

For every
i\in I^{v}
, let
B_{i}^{S}:=\varphi_{Z}(D_{i}^{Z})
(with the reduced structure).
For a subset
K\subset I^{v}
, we denote by
B_{K}^{S}
the scheme-theoretic
intersection
\bigcap_{i\in K}B_{i}^{S}
.
Lemma
5.10
implies that

\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}^{-1}(B_{i}^{S})=D_{i}^{Z^{\prime}}
set-theoretically. Since

\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
is smooth and
B_{i}^{S}
is reduced, its inverse image is
reduced. Thus this equality is scheme-theoretic. Consequently,
inverse images commute with scheme-theoretic intersections and hence

\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}^{-1}(B_{K}^{S})=D_{K}^{Z^{\prime}}

scheme-theoretically. The pair
(Z^{\prime},D^{Z^{\prime}})
is snc, so for every
K\subset I^{v}
the intersection
D_{K}^{Z^{\prime}}
is smooth and, whenever
it is nonempty, it has codimension
|K|
in
Z^{\prime}
. Since

\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
is smooth this implies that
B_{K}^{S}
is smooth and

\operatorname{codim}_{S}B_{K}^{S}=\operatorname{codim}_{Z^{\prime}}D_{K}^{Z^{\prime}}=|K|.

In particular, every
B_{i}^{S}
is a smooth irreducible divisor, and
every nonempty intersection
B_{K}^{S}
is smooth of the expected
codimension. Therefore

B^{S}:=\sum_{i\in I^{v}}B_{i}^{S}
is a simple normal crossing divisor on

S
.

Step 2.
Assume that
J\subsetneq I^{h}
and that the assertion
holds for every subset properly containing
J
. Let

m:=|I^{h}\backslash J|
and set
Z^{\prime}:=D_{J}^{h}
. By Lemma

5.10
,

\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}:=\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\to S
is a good log contraction of

(Z^{\prime},D^{Z^{\prime}})
. By Proposition
5.5
, for
general
y\in S
we have

(Z^{\prime}_{y},D_{y}^{Z^{\prime}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r+m},H_{1}+\cdots+H_{m})
and

{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z^{\prime}_{y}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+m}}(1)
.

Let
F
be an arbitrary fiber of
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
and let
F_{0}
be an
irreducible component of
F
of maximal dimension. Since the general
fiber has dimension
r+m
, we have

\dim F_{0}\geq r+m
. If
F_{0}
were contained in a horizontal
component
D_{i_{0}}^{Z^{\prime}}
of
D^{Z^{\prime}}
, then the induction assumption
applied to
J\cup\{i_{0}\}
would give
\dim F_{0}\leq r+m-1
, a
contradiction. Thus
F_{0}
is not contained in any horizontal component
of
D^{Z^{\prime}}
.

Choose a projective curve
C^{\prime}\subset F_{0}
which is not contained in
any horizontal component of
D^{Z^{\prime}}
. By Proposition

1.5
, we can find an extremal rational curve

C
contracted by
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
, lying in the same fiber and in the
same stratum as
C^{\prime}
. In particular,
C
is not contained in any
horizontal component of
D^{Z^{\prime}}
. Let
f:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\to C
be the
normalization of
C
, let
K
be the set of all
j\in I^{v}
for which

C\subset D_{j}^{Z}
, and let
Z^{\prime\prime}
be the connected component of

D_{K}^{Z^{\prime}}
containing
C
.

By Lemma
5.10
,
S^{\prime\prime}:=\varphi_{Z}(Z^{\prime\prime})
is normal
and
\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}:=\varphi_{Z}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}}:Z^{\prime\prime}\to S^{\prime\prime}
is a good log
contraction of
(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})
. By Lemma

4.6
,
(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})
is
semi-contact, and Corollary
5.4
gives

\dim S^{\prime\prime}<\dim Z^{\prime\prime}
.

Let
D^{Z^{\prime\prime},v}
be the vertical part of
D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}
with respect to

\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
. By Lemma
5.10
, every component
of
D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}
induced by a vertical component of
D^{Z}
is vertical
with respect to
\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
. The components indexed by

I^{h}\backslash J
are horizontal, so the horizontal part

D^{Z^{\prime\prime},h}
has exactly
m
irreducible components.

Lemma
5.10
shows that
F\subset Z^{\prime\prime}
and that

C\subset U_{Z^{\prime\prime}}:=\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}^{-1}(U_{S^{\prime\prime}})
. If
\dim S^{\prime\prime}>0
,
Proposition
5.8
shows that the fiber of

\varphi_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
containing
C
has dimension
r+m
. Since this fiber
is
F
, we obtain
\dim F=r+m
.

Lemma 5.12
.

If
\dim S^{\prime\prime}=0
then
(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r+m},H_{1}+...+H_{m})
.

Proof.

Since
\dim S>0
, the set
K
is nonempty. Therefore

\dim Z^{\prime\prime}\leq\dim Z^{\prime}-1\leq\dim Z-1\leq 2r
.
Suppose that
\rho(Z^{\prime\prime})>1
. Clearly,
(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})
is log Fano and by Lemma

4.20
, the log Fano pseudoindex satisfies

\imath(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})=r+1>\frac{\dim Z^{\prime\prime}}{2}.

By
[
Fuj14
, Theorem 4.3]
, this would imply
\dim Z^{\prime\prime}=2\imath(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})-1=2r+1
,
contradicting
\dim Z^{\prime\prime}\leq 2r
. Therefore
\rho(Z^{\prime\prime})=1
. Then equality

-K_{Z^{\prime\prime}}=(r+1)c_{1}({\mathcal{L}}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}})+D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}

shows that
-K_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
is ample. Thus
Z^{\prime\prime}
is Fano and

{\mathop{\rm Pic\,}}Z^{\prime\prime}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}
. Moreover, if
C
is the extremal rational curve
used in the construction of
Z^{\prime\prime}
, then
{\mathcal{L}}\cdot C=1
. Therefore

{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}}
is the ample generator of
{\mathop{\rm Pic\,}}Z^{\prime\prime}
.
Since

{\mathcal{L}}\cdot C=1
and
D_{i}^{Z^{\prime}}\cdot C=0
for every
i\in K
, it follows that

{\mathcal{O}}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}(-D_{i}^{Z^{\prime}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}

for every
i\in K
.

Lemma
5.10
also shows that
Z^{\prime\prime}
is the geometric fiber of
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
over the point

\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}(Z^{\prime\prime})
. Since the boundary has simple normal crossings,

N^{*}_{Z^{\prime\prime}/Z^{\prime}}=I_{Z^{\prime\prime}}/I_{Z^{\prime\prime}}^{2}\simeq\bigoplus_{i\in K}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}(-D_{i}^{Z^{\prime}})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Z^{\prime\prime}}^{\oplus(\dim Z^{\prime}-\dim Z^{\prime\prime})}.

Since
Z^{\prime\prime}
is Fano, Kodaira vanishing gives

H^{1}(Z^{\prime\prime},{\mathcal{O}}_{Z^{\prime\prime}})=0
. Hence all the assumptions of Castelnuovo’s
criterion
[
AW97
, Proposition 3.8]
are
satisfied, and

\dim S=h^{0}(Z^{\prime\prime},N^{*}_{Z^{\prime\prime}/Z^{\prime}})=\dim Z^{\prime}-\dim Z^{\prime\prime}.

On the other hand, the general fiber of
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
has dimension

r+m
, so

\dim Z^{\prime\prime}=\dim Z^{\prime}-\dim S=r+m
.
Let us write
D_{i}^{Z^{\prime\prime}}=a_{i}c_{1}({\mathcal{L}}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}})
for
i\in I^{h}\backslash J
and some positive integers
a_{i}
. Then

-K_{Z^{\prime\prime}}=\left(r+1+\sum_{i\in I^{h}\backslash J}a_{i}\right)c_{1}({\mathcal{L}}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}}).

Since there are
m
integers
a_{i}\geq 1
, we have

r+1+\sum_{i\in I^{h}\backslash J}a_{i}\geq r+m+1=\dim Z^{\prime\prime}+1.

The Kobayashi–Ochiai theorem implies that equality holds. Therefore

a_{i}=1
for every
i\in I^{h}\backslash J
and

(Z^{\prime\prime},{\mathcal{L}}|_{Z^{\prime\prime}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r+m},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+m}}(1)).

The components of
D^{Z^{\prime\prime}}
are consequently hyperplanes, and hence

(Z^{\prime\prime},D^{Z^{\prime\prime}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r+m},H_{1}+\cdots+H_{m})
.
∎

If
\dim S^{\prime\prime}=0
then Lemma
5.10
shows that
Z^{\prime\prime}=F
.
So the above lemma implies that
\dim F=r+m
.
Thus all fibers of
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
have dimension
r+m
. Then Fujita’s
theorem
[
Fuj87
, Lemma 2.12]
implies that
\varphi_{Z^{\prime}}
is a projective bundle.

This completes the induction. In particular, for
J=\emptyset
the
map
\varphi_{Z}:Z\to S
is a projective bundle and hence is smooth.
Lemma
5.10
then gives, scheme-theoretically,

D_{i}^{Z}=\varphi_{Z}^{-1}(B_{i}^{S})
for every
i\in I^{v}
. The divisor

B^{S}
is the simple normal crossing divisor constructed in Step 1.
∎

Example 5.13
.

Let us fix some
1\leq s\leq r
, a projective snc pair
(S,B^{S})
.
Assume that
1\leq\dim S\leq(r-s+1)
. Let
\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}=({\mathcal{M}}_{1},...,{\mathcal{M}}_{s})
be a collection of

s
line bundles on
S
and let

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus s}\to{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{D}}_{T_{S}(-\log B^{S})}^{\leq 1}(\underline{{\mathcal{M}}})\to T_{S}(-\log B^{S})\to 0

be the extension defined in Subsection
3.4
, dual to the logarithmic multi-Atiyah extension of
{\mathcal{M}}

(so that
{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}})^{*}
).
Let us consider extensions

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus(r+1-s-\dim S)}\to{\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\to 0

(so that
{\mathcal{G}}
has rank
r+1
) and

0\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\oplus\gamma_{i}}{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}\to 0.

Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{i}
be the cokernel of
\gamma_{i}:{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{E}}
.
Then on
Z:={\mathbb{P}}_{S}({\mathcal{E}})
we can consider
s
horizontal divisors
D^{h}_{i}:={\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{G}}_{i})
. If
\varphi_{Z}:Z\to S
is the canonical projection then the divisor
D^{Z}:=\sum_{i=1}^{s}D^{h}_{i}+\varphi_{Z}^{-1}(B^{S})
has simple normal crossings.
