Note also that
\dim Z=\dim S+s+r
. In particular, if
\dim Z=2r+1
then
{\mathcal{G}}={\mathcal{C}}
.

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
(Z,D^{Z},{\mathcal{L}})
be a rank
(2r+1)
projective semi-contact snc pair. Let
\varphi_{Z}:Z\to S
be a very good elementary log contraction with
\dim S>0
. Then
S
is smooth,
{\mathcal{E}}=\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{L}}
is locally free,
(Z,{\mathcal{L}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})}(1))
, and there exists a simple normal crossing divisor
B^{S}
on
S
such that one of the following cases occur:

1.

{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}{\mathcal{E}}=r+1
,
D^{Z}=\varphi_{Z}^{-1}(B^{S})
and we have a short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus(r+1-\dim S)}\to{\mathcal{E}}\to T_{S}(-\log B^{S})\to 0.

2.

{\mathop{\rm rk\,}}{\mathcal{E}}=r+s+1
for some
1\leq s\leq r
, and
{\mathcal{E}}
and
D^{Z}
are as in Example
5.13
.

Proof.

Proposition
5.9
implies that
S
is smooth and
\varphi_{Z}
is a projective bundle.
So Proposition
5.5
implies that
{\mathcal{E}}=\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{L}}
is locally free and
(Z,{\mathcal{L}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}),{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})}(1))
. It also implies that there exists
0\leq s\leq r
such that
{\mathcal{E}}
has rank
(r+s+1)
and
D^{Z}
has exactly
s
horizontal components
D^{h}_{1},...,D_{s}^{h}
. The relative Euler exact sequence for
\varphi_{Z}
shows that

\omega_{Z/S}=\varphi_{Z}^{*}(\det{\mathcal{E}})\otimes{\mathcal{L}}^{-(r+s+1)}
and hence

\omega_{Z}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}^{r+s+1}=\varphi_{Z}^{*}(\omega_{S}\otimes\det{\mathcal{E}}).

Since
\omega_{Z}(D^{Z})\simeq{\mathcal{L}}^{-(r+1)}
, we get

{\mathcal{L}}^{s}(-D^{Z})\simeq\varphi_{Z}^{*}(\omega_{S}\otimes\det{\mathcal{E}}).

By Proposition
5.9
we also know that there exists a simple normal crossing divisor
B^{S}
on
S
such that

\varphi_{Z}^{-1}(B^{S})
is the vertical part of
D^{Z}
.
In the rest of the proof we set
n:=\dim Z
and
q:=2r+1-n
.
Since
n=\dim S+r+s
, we have
q=r+1-s-\dim S
.

Let
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
be a special contact Lie algebroid on
(Z,D^{Z})
.
Let us set
{\mathcal{B}}={\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathbb{L}}}({\mathcal{L}})
and consider the
{\mathbb{L}}
-Atiyah extension of
{\mathcal{L}}

0\to\Omega_{{\mathbb{L}}}^{1}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\to 0.

We also consider the logarithmic Atiyah bundle
{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}
of
{\mathcal{L}}
on

(Z,D^{Z})
and the relative logarithmic Atiyah bundle
{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}
of
{\mathcal{L}}
on

(Z,D^{Z})/(S,B^{S})
(see Subsection
3.7
).
By Lemma
3.13
we have short exact sequences

0\longrightarrow\varphi_{Z}^{*}\Omega_{S}^{1}(\log B^{S})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\longrightarrow 0

(10)

and

0\longrightarrow\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}(-1)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{i}^{h}}\longrightarrow 0,

(11)

where the direct sum in the second sequence is zero when
s=0
. Since
\varphi_{Z}
and every restriction
\varphi_{Z}|_{D_{i}^{h}}:D_{i}^{h}\to S
are projective bundles, the projection formula and standard vanishings give

R^{1}\varphi_{Z*}\bigl(\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}(-1)\bigr)={\mathcal{E}}\otimes R^{1}\varphi_{Z*}\bigl({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(-1)\bigr)=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad R^{1}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{i}^{h}}=0.

Therefore
R^{1}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}=0
.
Similarly,

R^{1}\varphi_{Z*}\bigl(\varphi_{Z}^{*}\Omega_{S}^{1}(\log B^{S})\bigr)\simeq\Omega_{S}^{1}(\log B^{S})\otimes R^{1}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}=0
,
so the first exact sequence gives

R^{1}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}=0.

Let us set
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}:=\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}:=\varphi_{Z*}({\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}(-1))
.
By Lemma
3.14
we have the short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}^{\oplus q}\to 0.

So pushing down the above sequence gives

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\longrightarrow\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus q}\longrightarrow 0.

Pushing down the dual of above sequence twisted by
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(-1)
gives
\varphi_{Z*}({\mathcal{B}}^{*}(-1))\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
.

Let us recall that by Proposition
3.9
existence of the contact structure on
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
implies existence of an
{\mathcal{L}}
-valued symplectic form on
{\mathcal{B}}^{*}
. In particular, we have an isomorphism

{\mathcal{B}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}^{*}(-1).
Therefore
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\simeq\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}
and we have a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus q}\longrightarrow 0.

(12)

It remains only to identify
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
. To do that we consider the following two cases.

Case 1:
s=0
.

In this case sequences (
10
) and (
11
) reduce to

0\longrightarrow\varphi_{Z}^{*}\Omega_{S}^{1}(\log B^{S})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\longrightarrow\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}(-1)\longrightarrow 0.

Pushing it down to
S
, we obtain a canonical isomorphism
\Omega^{1}_{S}(\log B^{S})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
.
Pushing down the dual of above sequence twisted by
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(-1)
gives
{\mathcal{E}}^{*}\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
.
So dualizing (
12
) we get the short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus q}\to{\mathcal{E}}\to T_{S}(-\log B^{S})\to 0.

Since
q=r+1-\dim S
, this gives the first case in the theorem.

Case 2:

1\leq s\leq r
.

Let us set
{\mathcal{M}}:=\omega_{S}\otimes\det{\mathcal{E}}(B^{S})
and
D^{h}=\sum_{i=1}^{s}D^{h}_{i}
. Then
{\mathcal{L}}^{s}(-D^{h})\simeq\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}
.

Let us set
J=\{1,...,s\}
and
{\mathcal{G}}=\varphi_{Z*}({\mathcal{L}}|_{D^{h}_{J}})
. Note that
{\mathcal{G}}
is locally free of rank
(r+1)
and
(D^{h}_{J},{\mathcal{L}}|_{D^{h}_{J}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{G}}),{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{G}})}(1))
.
By Subsection
3.8
there exist line bundles
{\mathcal{M}}_{i}
such that
{\mathcal{L}}(-D^{h}_{i})\simeq\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}
for
i=1,...,s
. Let
\gamma_{i}:{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{E}}=\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{L}}
be the canonical injective map corresponding to
\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{L}}
and let
{\mathcal{G}}_{i}
be the cokernel of
\gamma_{i}
. Then
{\mathcal{G}}_{i}

is locally free and the projection
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{G}}_{i}
defines the embedding
D^{h}_{i}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{G}}_{i})\hookrightarrow Z\simeq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Lemma 5.15
.

We have a short exact sequence

0\to\bigoplus_{i\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\oplus\gamma_{i}}{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{G}}\to 0.

Proof.

Note that if
H_{1},...,H_{s}
are hyperplanes in general position in
{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}
then

H^{1}(J_{H_{1}\cap...\cap H_{s}}(1))=0.

Hence

R^{1}\varphi_{Z*}(J_{D^{h}_{J}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})=0.

Similarly, vanishings

H^{0}(J_{H_{1}\cap...\cap H_{s}}^{2}(1))=H^{1}(J_{H_{1}\cap...\cap H_{s}}^{2}(1))=0

imply that

\varphi_{Z*}(J^{2}_{D_{J}^{h}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})=R^{1}\varphi_{Z*}(J^{2}_{D_{J}^{h}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})=0.

We claim that the required sequence is the push-forward of
the short exact sequence

0\to J_{D^{h}_{J}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}}|_{D^{h}_{J}}\to 0.

By the above we only need to check that
\varphi_{Z*}(J_{D^{h}_{J}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})\simeq\bigoplus_{i\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}

and the push-forward map is given by
\oplus_{i\in J}\gamma_{i}
.

The push-forward of the sequence

0\to J_{D^{h}_{J}}^{2}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\to J_{D^{h}_{J}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\to J_{D^{h}_{J}}/J_{D^{h}_{J}}^{2}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\to 0

gives an isomorphism
\varphi_{Z*}(J_{D^{h}_{J}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})\to\varphi_{Z*}(J_{D^{h}_{J}}/J_{D^{h}_{J}}^{2}\otimes{\mathcal{L}})
.
We also have a surjective map

\bigoplus_{i\in J}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(-D^{h}_{i})\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\simeq\varphi_{Z}^{*}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right)\to J_{D^{h}_{J}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}},

which after tensoring with
{\mathcal{O}}_{D^{h}_{J}}
gives an isomorphism

(\varphi_{Z}|_{D^{h}_{J}})^{*}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right)\to J_{D^{h}_{J}}/J_{D^{h}_{J}}^{2}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}.

Taking the pushforward gives the required assertions.
∎

The following lemma is a generalization of Lemma
3.15
. It also generalizes
[
DK93
, Proposition 2.10]
that corresponds to the case when
S
is a point
(our approach gives also a canonical sequence explaining the isomorphism).

Lemma 5.16
.

The relative evaluation map
\varphi_{Z}^{*}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}
is injective and its cokernel is isomorphic to
\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)
.
Moreover,
\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}
is canonically isomorphic to
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus s}
.

Proof.

The relative Atiyah bundle
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}
is isomorphic to
\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}(-1)
(this follows from the relative Euler sequence). So by Lemma
3.13
we have the following short exact sequence:

0\to\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}(-1)\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\to{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}}{\mathcal{O}}_{D^{h}_{i}}\to 0.

Pushing it down we see that
\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\to\varphi_{Z*}\left({\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}}{\mathcal{O}}_{D^{h}_{i}}\right)={\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus s}

is an isomorphism. So we have a well-defined map

{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(-D^{h}_{i})\to{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\simeq\varphi_{Z}^{*}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}.

On the other hand, the twisted pull-back of
\oplus\gamma_{i}
defines the map

{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(-D^{h}_{i})\simeq\varphi_{Z}^{*}\left(\bigoplus_{i\in J}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\right)(-1)\to\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}(-1),

whose cokernel is isomorphic to
\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)
by Lemma
5.15
.
Using the sum of these maps and the relative evaluation we can form the sequence

0\to{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(-D^{h}_{i})\to\varphi_{Z}^{*}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\oplus\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}(-1)\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\to 0.

It is exact as can be easily checked fiberwise on the fibers of
\varphi_{Z}
. Therefore
we have the induced short exact sequence

0\to\varphi_{Z}^{*}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\to\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)\to 0.

∎

The following lemma and its proof are analogous to that of Lemma
3.16
.

Lemma 5.17
.

Let
\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}=({\mathcal{M}}_{1},...,{\mathcal{M}}_{s})
and let

0\to\Omega^{1}_{S}(\log B^{S})\to{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus s}\to 0

be the logarithmic multi-Atiyah extension of
\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}
(see Subsection
3.7
).
Then we have a canonical short exact sequence

0\to\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\to\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)\to 0.

In particular,
\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}
and the first map can be identified with the relative evaluation map
\varphi_{Z}^{*}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}
.

Proof.

Since
{\mathcal{L}}(-D^{h}_{i})\simeq\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}
, as in the proof of Lemma
3.16

we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{Z}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{S}(\log B^{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d\varphi_{Z}}
\textstyle{\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{i}}
\textstyle{\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{Z}^{1}(\log D^{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

This gives rise to the commutative diagram

\textstyle{(\Omega^{1}_{S}(\log B^{S}))^{\oplus s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\bigoplus}_{i=1}^{s}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{M}}_{i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}\varphi_{Z*}\alpha_{i}}
\textstyle{(\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}})^{\oplus s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{S}(\log B^{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}}

in which the first and third vertical arrows are codiagonals and
the dotted arrow exists by the universal property of the pushout (note that the top curved arrow does not depend on
\alpha_{i}
and it is equal to the direct sum of
s
copies of the bottom curved arrow).
So by construction and Lemma
3.13
we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{Z}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{S}(\log B^{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{Z}^{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus s})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{Z}^{*}\Omega^{1}_{S}(\log B^{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{{{\mathcal{L}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{{{\mathcal{L}}}/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

in which the last vertical arrow can be identified with the relative evaluation map
\varphi_{Z}^{*}\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}/S}
. So by Lemma
5.16

we get a short exact sequence

0\to\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\to\varphi_{Z}^{*}{\mathcal{G}}(-1)\to 0.

Taking pushforward we get
{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}\simeq\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}
, which implies also the last part of the lemma.
∎

Lemma
5.17
shows that

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}=\varphi_{Z*}{\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}.

Dualizing the sequence from Lemma
5.17
, twisting it by
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(-1)
and pushing it down to
S
, we obtain

{\mathcal{F}}_{2}=\varphi_{Z*}({\mathcal{B}}_{{\mathcal{L}}}^{*}(-1))\simeq{\mathcal{G}}^{*}.

Therefore, dualizing sequence (
12
), we obtain a short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus q}\to{\mathcal{G}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}^{*}\to 0.

By definition,
{\mathcal{B}}_{\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}}^{*}={\mathcal{D}}_{T_{S}(-\log B^{S})}^{\leq 1}(\underline{{\mathcal{M}}})={\mathcal{C}}
, and
q=r+1-s-\dim S
. Thus we obtain the first extension from Example
5.13
. Lemma
5.15
gives the second extension, and the description
D^{Z}=\sum_{i=1}^{s}D_{i}^{h}+\varphi_{Z}^{-1}(B^{S})
, where
D_{i}^{h}={\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{G}}_{i})
, identifies the boundary with the divisor from that example. This proves the second case and finishes the proof of Theorem
5.14
.
∎

As a corollary we get the following classification of semi-contact varieties:

Corollary 5.18
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective semi-contact variety of rank
(2r+1)
.
Then one of the following occurs:

1.

X
is contact and
\dim X=2r+1
,

2.

X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
,

3.

K_{X}\sim 0
and
r\leq\dim X\leq 2r
,

4.

r+1\leq\dim X\leq 2r
and there exist a smooth projective variety
S
with
1\leq\dim S\leq r
and a vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
of rank
r+1
on
S
fitting into a short
exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus(r+1-\dim S)}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow T_{S}\longrightarrow 0

such that

X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}_{S}({\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof.

Let
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
be a special contact Lie algebroid of rank
(2r+1)
on
X
.

If
K_{X}
is pseudoeffective then Corollary
4.14

implies that
K_{X}\sim 0
and
\dim X<2r+1
. On the other hand, Lemma
4.9
gives
r\leq\dim X
,
so we are in case (3).

Now assume that
K_{X}
is not pseudoeffective and hence
K_{X}
is not nef.

If
b_{2}(X)=1
then Proposition
4.7
, applied with
D=0
, gives two possibilities. In the first one,
\dim X=2r+1
and
X
is contact, which is case (1). In the second one, for some
0\leq s\leq r
,
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r+s}

and
D
is an arrangement of
s
hyperplanes in general position. Since
D=0
, we must have
s=0
, and hence
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
.
Thus case (2) occurs.

If
b_{2}(X)\geq 2
then by the cone theorem, there exists an elementary contraction
\varphi\colon X\longrightarrow S

with
\dim S>0
. Since the boundary is empty, we can apply Theorem
5.14
.
Case (2) of that theorem cannot occur, because in that case the boundary contains at least one horizontal divisor. Therefore we are in case (1). It follows that
S
is smooth,
{\mathcal{E}}:=\varphi_{*}{\mathcal{L}}
is locally free of rank
r+1
,
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}_{S}({\mathcal{E}})

and there is a short exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus(r+1-\dim S)}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow T_{S}\longrightarrow 0.

In particular,
\dim S\leq r+1
. If
\dim S=r+1
, then
\dim X=2r+1
and case (1) occurs.
Otherwise, we have
1\leq\dim S\leq r
and hence
r+1\leq\dim X\leq 2r
. This is precisely case (4).
∎

Clearly, case (1) occurs. Case (2) occurs by Example
4.8
. Example
4.15

shows that case (3) is realized. To see that case (4) also occurs let us prove the following lemma (generalizing example (3) from Proposition
7.1
).

Lemma 5.19
.

For every
1\leq d\leq r
, the variety

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}(1)^{\oplus(d+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}^{\oplus(r-d)}\right)

is semi-contact of rank
(2r+1)
.

Proof.

If
d=r
then
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}(1)^{\oplus(d+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}^{\oplus(r-d)}\right)\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
. This variety is semi-contact of rank
(2r+1)
by Proposition
7.1
that will be proven later.
So in the following we assume that
1\leq d\leq r-1
. Let us set

k:=r+1-d\geq 2
and
S:={\mathbb{P}}^{d}.

Choose line bundles
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}:={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}(1)
,

{\mathcal{M}}_{2}=\cdots={\mathcal{M}}_{k}:={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}

and consider the smooth projective variety

q\colon Y:={\mathbb{P}}_{S}({\mathcal{O}}_{S}\oplus{\mathcal{M}}_{1})\times_{S}\cdots\times_{S}{\mathbb{P}}_{S}({\mathcal{O}}_{S}\oplus{\mathcal{M}}_{k})\longrightarrow S.

Note that
\dim Y=\dim S+k=r+1
.

For every
1\leq i\leq k
, let
S\longrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{S}({\mathcal{O}}_{S}\oplus{\mathcal{M}}_{i})

be the section corresponding to the quotient
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\oplus{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}_{i}
,
and let
B_{i}\subset Y
be its inverse image under the projection onto the

i
-th factor. Let us set

B:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{i}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Sigma:=\bigcap_{i=1}^{k}B_{i}.

Then
(Y,B)
is an snc pair and the restriction of
q
to
\Sigma
induces an
isomorphism
q|_{\Sigma}\colon\Sigma\xrightarrow{\sim}S
.
We use this isomorphism to identify
\Sigma
with
S
.

The restriction of the logarithmic tangent bundle to
S
fits into the
logarithmic normal exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{S}^{\oplus k}\longrightarrow T_{Y}(-\log B)|_{S}\longrightarrow T_{S}\longrightarrow 0.

The class of this extension is

\bigl(c_{1}({\mathcal{M}}_{1}),\ldots,c_{1}({\mathcal{M}}_{k})\bigr)=\bigl(c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}(1)),0,\ldots,0\bigr)\in H^{1}(S,\Omega_{S}^{1})^{\oplus k}.

Therefore

{\mathcal{E}}:=T_{Y}(-\log B)|_{S}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}(1)^{\oplus(d+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}^{\oplus(r-d)}.

More precisely, the nontrivial component of the above extension is the
Euler sequence, whereas the remaining
r-d
components are trivial.

By Proposition
6.1
the snc pair
(\tilde{X},\tilde{D}):=\bigl({\mathbb{P}}\bigl(T_{Y}(-\log B)\bigr),\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(B)\bigr)

is contact. Let
\tilde{\pi}\colon\tilde{X}\longrightarrow Y
be the canonical projection. The inverse image of the stratum
S
is

X:=\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(S)\simeq{\mathbb{P}}_{S}\bigl(T_{Y}(-\log B)|_{S}\bigr)={\mathbb{P}}_{S}({\mathcal{E}})\simeq{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}(1)^{\oplus(d+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{d}}^{\oplus(r-d)}\right).

Since
B_{1},\ldots,B_{k}
are all the irreducible components of
B
, the
boundary induced on
X
is empty. By restriction of the contact Lie
algebroid on
(\tilde{X},\tilde{D})
to this stratum,
X
is a smooth
projective semi-contact variety of rank
(2r+1)
.
∎

6
Constructions of contact snc pairs

In this section we give some examples of contact snc pairs. It is well known that a Mori contraction of a projective contact complex manifold is of fiber type (see
[
KPSW00
, Lemma 2.10]
). In fact, any contact smooth projective variety with
b_{2}\geq 2
is the projectivization of the tangent bundle over a smooth projective variety (see
[
KPSW00
, Theorem 2.12]
). Similarly, the projectivization of the logarithmic tangent bundle over a projective snc pair with a natural simple normal crossing divisor carries a canonical contact structure. Below we describe this construction in more detail and we call it the standard contact log structure. However, in the logarithmic case there also exist more complicated contact structures that live on projectivizations of some special vector bundles on lower dimensional varieties.

In this section we do not need to assume that varieties are projective.

6.1
Standard contact log structure

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
r\geq 1
and let
(Y,B)
be an snc pair, where
\dim Y=r+1
. Let
\pi:X={\mathbb{P}}(T_{Y}(-\log B))\to Y

and let
D=\pi^{-1}(B)
. Then the snc pair
(X,D)
carries a canonical contact structure. Moreover,
\pi
is a good elementary log contraction of
(X,D)
.

Proof.

Let us set
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1):={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}(T_{Y}(-\log B))}(1)
. Let us consider the snc pair consisting of the total space of the logarithmic cotangent bundle
\tilde{\pi}:\tilde{X}={\mathbb{T}}^{*}Y(-\log B)={\mathbb{V}}(T_{Y}(-\log B))\to Y

with the divisor
\tilde{D}:=\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(B)
. Let

\tilde{\eta}\in H^{0}\left(\tilde{X},\Omega_{\tilde{X}}^{1}(\log\tilde{D})\right)

be the canonical logarithmic Liouville form. Intrinsically, if

\alpha\in\Omega_{Y}^{1}(\log B)_{y}
is regarded as a point of

\tilde{X}
, then

\tilde{\eta}_{\alpha}(v)=\alpha(d\tilde{\pi}(v)).

Then
\tilde{\omega}:=d\tilde{\eta}
is a canonical logarithmic symplectic form on

(\tilde{X},\tilde{D})
.
Indeed, the vertical tangent bundle
T_{\tilde{X}/Y}
of
\tilde{\pi}
is canonically isomorphic to

\tilde{\pi}^{*}\Omega_{Y}^{1}(\log B).

If
v_{\beta}
is the vertical tangent vector corresponding to

\beta\in\Omega_{Y}^{1}(\log B)_{y}
, and
w
is a logarithmic tangent
vector whose image in
T_{Y}(-\log B)_{y}
is
u
, then

\tilde{\omega}(v_{\beta},w)=\beta(u),

up to the sign determined by the convention for the Liouville form.
Therefore the perfect pairing

\Omega_{Y}^{1}(\log B)\otimes T_{Y}(-\log B)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}

shows that
\tilde{\omega}
is nondegenerate.

Let
\tilde{X}^{\circ}
be the complement of the zero section in
\tilde{X}
and let
p:\tilde{X}^{\circ}\to X

denote the canonical projection. Note that
p
is the principal
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle associated with
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-1)
. If
h_{a}
denotes multiplication by
a\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
in the fibers of
\tilde{X}\to Y
, then

h_{a}^{*}\tilde{\eta}=a\tilde{\eta}\qquad\text{and}\qquad h_{a}^{*}\tilde{\omega}=a\tilde{\omega}.

Thus
\tilde{\omega}|_{\tilde{X}^{\circ}}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant. So Corollary
3.12
gives a canonical
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)
-valued contact structure on
T_{X}(-\log D)
. The last assertion concerning
\pi
follows from the fact that
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-(K_{X}+D))\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(r+1)
is
\pi
-ample and
N^{1}(X/Y)
is generated by
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)
.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

One can easily see that the canonical contact structure
T_{X}(-\log D)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)

is obtained as the composition of
d\pi:T_{X}(-\log D)\to\pi^{*}T_{Y}(-\log B)
with the tautological quotient map
\pi^{*}T_{Y}(-\log B)\xrightarrow{\theta}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)
.

6.2
Non-standard contact log structures

Proposition 6.3
.

Let us fix positive integers
1\leq s\leq r
.
Let
(Y,B)
be an snc pair, where
\dim Y=r-s+1
, and let
{\mathcal{M}}_{1},\ldots,{\mathcal{M}}_{s}
be line bundles on
Y
. Let

{\mathcal{C}}
be the canonical extension of
T_{Y}(-\log B)
by
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{\oplus s}
with extension class

-\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}c_{1}^{\log}({\mathcal{M}}_{i}).

Let
{\mathcal{E}}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\oplus{\mathcal{C}}
and let
\pi:X={\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})\to Y
. Let
D^{h}_{j}
be the divisor corresponding to the projection

{\mathcal{E}}\to\bigoplus_{i\neq j}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}\oplus{\mathcal{C}}

and let
D=\sum_{j=1}^{s}D^{h}_{j}+\pi^{*}B
. Then the snc pair
(X,D)
carries a canonical contact structure. Moreover,
\pi
is a good elementary log contraction of
(X,D)
.

Proof.

Let us set
\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s}{\mathcal{M}}_{i}
and consider the total space

q:Z:=\mathbb{V}_{Y}(\underline{{\mathcal{M}}})\longrightarrow Y.

Thus the fibers of
Z
are the duals of the fibers of
\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}
. For every
i
, let
H_{i}\subset Z
be the zero divisor of the
i
-th fiber coordinate, and put
A:=\sum_{i=1}^{s}H_{i}+q^{-1}(B)
.
Then
(Z,A)
is an snc pair and

\dim Z=\dim Y+s=r+1.

We first identify the logarithmic tangent bundle of
(Z,A)
. The logarithmic relative tangent sequence is

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}^{\oplus s}\longrightarrow T_{Z}(-\log A)\longrightarrow q^{*}T_{Y}(-\log B)\longrightarrow 0,

where the
i
-th standard basis vector in
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}^{\oplus s}
corresponds to the Euler vector field
\epsilon_{i}
along the
i
-th line-bundle factor. By definition, we have

{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{D}}_{T_{Y}(-\log B)}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{M}}_{1})\times_{T_{Y}(-\log B)}\cdots\times_{T_{Y}(-\log B)}{\mathcal{D}}_{T_{Y}(-\log B)}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{M}}_{s}).

Thus
{\mathcal{C}}
acts naturally on the total space
Z
: an element of
{\mathcal{D}}_{T_{Y}(-\log B)}^{\leq 1}({\mathcal{M}}_{i})
acts on
{\mathcal{M}}_{i}^{-1}
and hence induces a fiberwise linear vector field on
\mathbb{V}_{Y}({\mathcal{M}}_{i})
, while the common symbol gives its projection to
T_{Y}(-\log B)
. These vector fields preserve the divisors
H_{i}
and
q^{-1}(B)
. Therefore we obtain a canonical morphism

q^{*}{\mathcal{C}}\to T_{Z}(-\log A),

which fits into the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>q^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{\oplus s}@>{}>{}>q^{*}{\mathcal{C}}@>{}>{}>q^{*}T_{Y}(-\log B)@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{1_{i}\mapsto-\epsilon_{i}}V{\simeq}V@V{}V{}V@V{\mathrm{id}}V{}V\\
0@>{}>{}>{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}^{\oplus s}@>{}>{}>T_{Z}(-\log A)@>{}>{}>q^{*}T_{Y}(-\log B)@>{}>{}>0.\end{CD}

Indeed, the identity operator on
{\mathcal{M}}_{i}^{-1}
induces minus the Euler vector field
\epsilon_{i}
, because
\epsilon_{i}
acts as multiplication by
-1
on functions of weight
-1
. Hence we have a canonical isomorphism

q^{*}{\mathcal{C}}\simeq T_{Z}(-\log A).

Now let us consider
\tilde{X}:=\mathbb{V}_{Y}({\mathcal{E}}).

The direct-sum decomposition
{\mathcal{E}}=\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}\oplus{\mathcal{C}}
gives a projection
\rho:\tilde{X}\to Z
, and the preceding identification gives canonical isomorphisms

\tilde{X}\simeq\mathbb{V}_{Z}(q^{*}{\mathcal{C}})\simeq\mathbb{V}_{Z}\bigl(T_{Z}(-\log A)\bigr),

i.e.,
\tilde{X}
is canonically the total space of the logarithmic cotangent bundle of
(Z,A)
(here we use the assumption that
{\mathcal{E}}
is the direct sum
\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}\oplus{\mathcal{C}}
and not just an extension of
{\mathcal{C}}
by
\underline{{\mathcal{M}}}
). Let

\tilde{D}:=\rho^{-1}(A)

and let

\tilde{\eta}\in H^{0}\left(\tilde{X},\Omega_{\tilde{X}}^{1}(\log\tilde{D})\right)

be the canonical logarithmic Liouville form. As in the proof of Proposition
6.1
, its exterior derivative

\tilde{\omega}:=d\tilde{\eta}

is the canonical logarithmic symplectic form on the logarithmic cotangent bundle of
(Z,A)
.

We claim that
\tilde{\omega}
is homogeneous of weight
1
. Indeed, let
m_{a}:Z\to Z
be scalar multiplication by
a\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
in the fibers of
Z=\mathbb{V}_{Y}(\underline{{\mathcal{M}}})
. Under the identification of
\tilde{X}
with the logarithmic cotangent bundle of
Z
, the scalar action on
\tilde{X}=\mathbb{V}_{Y}({\mathcal{E}})
is the composition of the logarithmic cotangent lift of
m_{a}
and multiplication by
a
in the cotangent fibers. Cotangent lifts preserve the logarithmic Liouville form, whereas fiberwise multiplication by
a
multiplies it by
a
. Hence

a^{*}\tilde{\eta}=a\tilde{\eta}\qquad\text{and}\qquad a^{*}\tilde{\omega}=a\tilde{\omega}.

Let
\tilde{X}^{\circ}
be the complement of the zero section in
\tilde{X}
and let

p:\tilde{X}^{\circ}\to X

denote the canonical projection. Then
p
is the principal
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle associated with
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-1)
and

\tilde{D}|_{\tilde{X}^{\circ}}=p^{-1}(D).

Therefore Corollary
3.12
applied to the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariant logarithmic symplectic form
\tilde{\omega}|_{\tilde{X}^{\circ}}
induces a canonical
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)
-valued contact structure on
T_{X}(-\log D)
.
The last part comes from the fact that
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-(K_{X}+D))\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(r+1)
is
\pi
-ample and
N^{1}(X/Y)
is generated by
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)
.
∎

The following two examples show that in Theorem
0.1
a non-split extension

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow 0

can define a logarithmic contact pair, but an arbitrary non-split extension need not lead to such a structure.
The problem with generalization of construction from the proof of Proposition
6.3
is that
\rho:\mathbb{V}_{Y}({\mathcal{E}})\to Z

is in general only a torsor under
\mathbb{V}_{Z}(q^{*}{\mathcal{C}})\simeq{\mathbb{T}}_{Z}^{*}(\log A).

So it has no canonical identification with the logarithmic cotangent bundle
and therefore carries no canonical Liouville form. Local Liouville forms
may fail to glue, and the obstruction to gluing is controlled by the
extension cocycle. This explains why some non-split extensions still give
rise to logarithmic contact structures, whereas others do not.

Example 6.4

(
A non-split extension defining a log contact snc pair
)
.

Let
Y
be a complex elliptic curve, let
B=0
, and set
r=s=1
and
{\mathcal{M}}={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
. Then

{\mathcal{C}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\oplus T_{Y}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{\oplus 2}.

Choose a nonzero class

\xi\in H^{1}(Y,{\mathcal{O}}_{Y})
and let us represent it by a Čech cocycle
\{f_{ij}\}
.
Let us consider
a (non-split) extension

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow 0.

(13)

defined by the class
\beta\in H^{1}(Y,{\mathcal{C}}^{*})=H^{1}(Y,{\mathcal{O}}_{Y})\oplus H^{1}(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{1})

of the cocycle
(f_{ij},df_{ij})
. The class of
(df_{ij})
in
H^{1}(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{1})
vanishes by degeneration of the Hodge-to-de Rham spectral sequence and hence
\beta=(\xi,0)
but the choice of the cocycle helps to obtain convenient local fiber coordinates.

Let
\tilde{\pi}:\tilde{X}:={\mathbb{V}}_{Y}({\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow Y.
Let us choose an affine open covering
\{U_{i}\}
so that on each
U_{i}
we have a local coordinate
y_{i}
and a local splitting of (
13
). Let

\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}
be the local generator of
T_{Y}|_{U_{i}}
. Let

(y_{i};z_{i},t_{i},x_{i})

be the local coordinates on
\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(U_{i})
, where
z_{i}
,
t_{i}
are the fiber coordinates dual to the local generators of
{\mathcal{M}}
,
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\subset{\mathcal{C}}
,
and
x_{i}
corresponds to

\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}\in T_{Y}\subset{\mathcal{C}}
.
In particular, the divisor
\tilde{D}:={\mathbb{V}}_{Y}({\mathcal{C}})\subset\tilde{X}
is locally given by
z_{i}=0
.
Then we have the overlap formulas

z_{i}=z_{j},\qquad t_{i}=t_{j}+f_{ij}z_{j},\qquad x_{i}=\frac{\partial y_{j}}{\partial y_{i}}x_{j}+\frac{\partial f_{ij}}{\partial y_{i}}z_{j}.

(14)

In particular,

x_{i}\,dy_{i}=x_{j}\,dy_{j}+z_{j}\,df_{ij}.

On
\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(U_{i})
define

\eta_{i}:=t_{i}\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}-dt_{i}+x_{i}\,dy_{i}.

(15)

Using (
14
), we have

\eta_{i}=(t_{j}+f_{ij}z_{j})\frac{dz_{j}}{z_{j}}-d(t_{j}+f_{ij}z_{j})+x_{j}\,dy_{j}+z_{j}\,df_{ij}=t_{j}\frac{dz_{j}}{z_{j}}-dt_{j}+x_{j}\,dy_{j}=\eta_{j}.

Thus the local forms (
15
) glue to a global
logarithmic one-form

\eta\in H^{0}\bigl(\tilde{X},\Omega_{\tilde{X}}^{1}(\log\tilde{D})\bigr).

Let us set

\tilde{\omega}:=d\eta.

On
\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(U_{i})
we have

\tilde{\omega}=-\frac{1}{z_{i}}dz_{i}\wedge dt_{i}+dx_{i}\wedge dy_{i}

and hence

\tilde{\omega}^{\wedge 2}=-\frac{2}{z_{i}}dz_{i}\wedge dt_{i}\wedge dx_{i}\wedge dy_{i}.

Therefore
\tilde{\omega}
is a logarithmic symplectic form on

(\tilde{X},\tilde{D})
.

Let
\tilde{X}^{0}
be the complement of the zero section in
\tilde{X}
, and
let

p:\tilde{X}^{0}\longrightarrow X:={\mathbb{P}}_{Y}({\mathcal{E}})

be the canonical projection. The scalar
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action is locally given by

u\cdot(y_{i};z_{i},t_{i},x_{i})=(y_{i};uz_{i},ut_{i},ux_{i}).

So
\tilde{\omega}|_{\tilde{X}^{0}}
is
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-equivariant and hence by Corollary
3.12

it gives rise to an
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)
-valued contact structure on
(X,D)
, where
D:={\mathbb{P}}_{Y}({\mathcal{C}})
(cf. the proof of Proposition
6.1
). Since we use local coordinates, it is easy to write down the corresponding contact form explicitly. Namely, the restriction of
\eta
to
\tilde{X}^{0}
has weight
1
and it is horizontal for the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action. Hence it descends to an
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)
-valued logarithmic one-form
\theta
on
(X,D)
, where
D:={\mathbb{P}}_{Y}({\mathcal{C}})
. Since
\tilde{\omega}|_{\tilde{X}^{0}}=d(p^{*}\theta)

is nondegenerate,
\theta
is a contact form on
(X,D)
.
Finally, note that
\pi:X\to Y
is an elementary log contraction and its restriction to

X\setminus D
is not an isomorphism.

Example 6.5

(
A non-split extension which does not give rise to a log contact pair
)
.

Let
Y
be a smooth complex projective curve of genus
g\geq 2
, let
B=0
,
and again set
r=s=1
and
{\mathcal{M}}={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
. Then

{\mathcal{C}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\oplus T_{Y}.

Choose a nonzero class

0\neq\zeta\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(T_{Y},{\mathcal{O}}_{Y})=H^{1}(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{1}),

and let

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{V}}\longrightarrow T_{Y}\longrightarrow 0

(16)

be the corresponding non-split extension. Set

{\mathcal{E}}:={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\oplus{\mathcal{V}},

and embed
{\mathcal{M}}={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
as the subbundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\subset{\mathcal{V}}
. This gives a
non-split extension

0\longrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\oplus T_{Y}={\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow 0.

(17)

Set
X:={\mathbb{P}}_{Y}({\mathcal{E}})
,
D:={\mathbb{P}}_{Y}({\mathcal{C}})
, and let
\pi:X\to Y
be the projection. We claim that the snc pair
(X,D)

does not carry any contact structure.

Let
\tilde{\pi}:\tilde{X}:={\mathbb{V}}_{Y}({\mathcal{E}})\to Y,

\tilde{D}:={\mathbb{V}}_{Y}({\mathcal{C}}),

and choose an affine open covering
\{U_{i}\}
with local coordinates
y_{i}

and local splittings of (
16
). Represent
\zeta
by a
Čech cocycle

\gamma_{ij}\,dy_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{ij},\Omega_{Y}^{1}).

Let
(y_{i};z_{i},t_{i},x_{i})

be the corresponding coordinates on
\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(U_{i})
, where
z_{i}

corresponds to
{\mathcal{M}}\subset{\mathcal{V}}
,
t_{i}
to the direct summand

{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\subset{\mathcal{E}}
, and
x_{i}
to a local lift of

\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}
to
{\mathcal{V}}
. On overlaps we have

z_{i}=z_{j},\qquad t_{i}=t_{j},\qquad x_{i}=\frac{\partial y_{j}}{\partial y_{i}}x_{j}+\gamma_{ij}z_{j}.

(18)

The divisor
\tilde{D}
is locally given by
z_{i}=0
.

Since
\det{\mathcal{E}}\simeq T_{Y}
and

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(D)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)
, we have
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-(K_{X}+D))\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(2)
.
Assume
(X,D)
carries a contact structure
\theta:T_{X}(-\log D)\to{\mathcal{L}}
. Then

{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes 2}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(2)
,
and consequently
{\mathcal{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)\otimes\pi^{*}{\mathcal{N}}

for some
{\mathcal{N}}\in\operatorname{Pic}(Y)[2]
.
Using

\Omega_{X/Y}^{1}(\log D)\simeq\pi^{*}{\mathcal{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(-1),

the relative logarithmic cotangent sequence gives

0\longrightarrow\pi^{*}(\Omega_{Y}^{1}\otimes{\mathcal{N}})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(1)\longrightarrow\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log D)\otimes{\mathcal{L}}\longrightarrow\pi^{*}({\mathcal{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{N}})\longrightarrow 0.

(19)

Therefore the contact form
\theta^{*}
induces
\bar{\omega}\in H^{0}(Y,{\mathcal{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{N}}).

Suppose first that
{\mathcal{N}}\neq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
. Then
H^{0}(Y,{\mathcal{N}})=0
and
H^{0}(Y,T_{Y}\otimes{\mathcal{N}})=0
, so
\bar{\omega}=0
. It follows that the rank-two integrable bundle
T_{X/Y}(-\log D)
coincides with the kernel of the contact form
\theta
, a contradiction.

We can therefore assume that
{\mathcal{N}}={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
. Since
H^{0}(Y,T_{Y})=0
, the relative
component
\bar{\omega}
of a contact form is a nonzero multiple of

(1,0)\in H^{0}(Y,{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\oplus T_{Y}).

After rescaling, we can assume that it is equal to
(1,0)
. Let

p:\tilde{X}^{0}\longrightarrow X

be the canonical
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-torsor. On
\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(U_{i})
, the pullback of
the contact form must then have the form

\eta_{i}=t_{i}\frac{dz_{i}}{z_{i}}-dt_{i}+(a_{i}z_{i}+b_{i}t_{i}+c_{i}x_{i})\,dy_{i}

(20)

for some regular functions
a_{i},b_{i},c_{i}
on
U_{i}
.
Taking the exterior derivative of (
20
), we
obtain

d\eta_{i}=-\frac{1}{z_{i}}dz_{i}\wedge dt_{i}+(a_{i}\,dz_{i}+b_{i}\,dt_{i}+c_{i}\,dx_{i})\wedge dy_{i},

because all terms containing the wedge product of two differentials from the
one-dimensional base vanish. Hence

(d\eta_{i})^{\wedge 2}=-\frac{2c_{i}}{z_{i}}dz_{i}\wedge dt_{i}\wedge dx_{i}\wedge dy_{i}.

(21)

The contact condition therefore forces every
c_{i}
to be invertible.

The horizontal terms in (
20
) glue to a
section

h\in H^{0}(Y,\Omega_{Y}^{1}\otimes{\mathcal{E}})=\operatorname{Hom}(T_{Y},{\mathcal{E}}).

In the coordinates above,

h\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}\right)=a_{i}z_{i}+b_{i}t_{i}+c_{i}x_{i}.

The functions
c_{i}
are the local expressions of the endomorphism

T_{Y}\xrightarrow{h}{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow T_{Y}.

By (
21
), this endomorphism is an isomorphism.
Projecting
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\oplus{\mathcal{V}}
onto
{\mathcal{V}}
therefore produces a splitting
of (
16
), contrary to
\zeta\neq 0
. Thus
(X,D)
admits no
logarithmic contact structure.

7
Semi-contact snc pairs with two log contractions

In this section we describe structure of semi-contact snc pairs with two log contractions. To do so we first need to classify semi-contact log Fano varieties.

7.1
Semi-contact log Fano varieties

Using Theorem
5.14
one can classify semi-contact snc pairs with two log contractions.
Let us first note the following generalization of LeBrun–Salamon’s result
[
LS94
, Corollary 4.2]
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
(Z,D^{Z})
be a rank
(2r+1)
semi-contact log Fano variety with
b_{2}(Z)\geq 2
. Then one of the following occurs:

1.

(Z,D^{Z})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}(T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+1}}),0)
.

2.

For some
m\geq 1
,
Z\simeq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(m))
and
D^{Z}
corresponds to the projection

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(m)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}.

3.

(Z,D^{Z})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r},0)
.

Moreover, each of the above pairs is a semi-contact log Fano variety (with exactly two good elementary log contractions).

Proof.

If
D^{Z}\neq 0
then by Lemma
4.20
,
[
Fuj14
, Theorem 4.3]
shows that
(Z,D^{Z})
is as in (2). The main problem is to show that such pair
(Z,D^{Z})
has a contact structure.
Let us set
S={\mathbb{P}}^{r}
and
{\mathcal{M}}:={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(m)
. Since
m\neq 0
, the Atiyah extension of
{\mathcal{M}}
is non-trivial and hence
{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathcal{M}}}={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(-1)^{\oplus(r+1)}
. Let

{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}^{\oplus(r+1)}(1)\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(m)
and let us consider
\pi:Z={\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})\to S
with the divisor
D^{Z}={\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}^{\oplus(r+1)}(1))

corresponding to the projection
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}^{\oplus(r+1)}(1)
.
Then
(Z,D^{Z})
has a contact structure by
Proposition
6.3
.

Now assume that
D^{Z}=0
. If
\dim Z=2r+1
then
Z\simeq{\mathbb{P}}(T_{{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+1}}})
by
[
LS94
, Corollary 4.2]
. If
\dim Z\leq 2r
then by
[
Wiś90
, Theorem B]
we get
Z\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
. This variety is semi-contact, since it is the boundary in the contact snc pair
({\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+1}},{\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r})
(or in any variety from case (2)).

Note that in all the cases we have
b_{2}(Z)=2
. So, since
(Z,D^{Z})
is log Fano, it has exactly two good elementary log contractions.
∎

Example 7.2
.

It is useful to explicitly describe contractions of contact snc pairs in case (3).
(Z,D^{Z})
admits exactly two good elementary log contractions, one of which is given by the projectivization
\varphi_{1}
, so it is of fiber type.

If
m=1
then
Z={\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+1}}
and
D^{Z}={\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
is a divisor of type
(0,1)
. The second log contraction is the projection onto the second factor and it realizes
Z
as
{\mathbb{P}}(T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r+1}}(-\log H))
for the hyperplane
H\subset{\mathbb{P}}^{r+1}
, which is the image of
D^{Z}
.

If
m>1
then the second log contraction is the blow up of the cone in
{\mathbb{P}}^{\binom{m+r}{m}+r}

with vertex
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
over the
m
-th Veronese embedding
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{{\binom{m+r}{m}}-1}
.
This log contraction is divisorial and it contracts
D^{Z}={\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
to the vertex of the cone (the restriction to
D^{Z}
is projection onto the second factor of the product). Note that in this case
Z
is not isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}(T_{Y}(-\log B))
for any
(Y,B)
.

7.2
Semi-contact snc pairs with two contractions

The following theorem gives a generalization of
[
KPSW00
, Proposition 2.13]
to the semi-contact case.

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
(Z,D^{Z})
be a rank
(2r+1)
semi-contact projective snc pair that admits two good elementary log contractions

\varphi_{1}:Z\to S_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}:Z\to S_{2}
. Assume that
\varphi_{1}
is very good and
(Z,D^{Z})
is not log Fano.
Then there exist

1.

an
(r+1)
-dimensional projective variety
S
smooth outside a closed point
s_{0}\in S
at which

\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{S,s_{0}}
is isomorphic to the completion of the cone over the
m
-th Veronese embedding of
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
at the vertex for some
m\geq 1
, and

2.

a projective simple normal crossing divisor
B
on
S\backslash\{s_{0}\}
,

such that
if
\psi_{1}:S_{1}\to S
is the blow up of
S
at
s_{0}
with exceptional divisor
E
then

(\varphi_{1}:Z\to S_{1})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}(T_{S_{1}}(-\log(\psi_{1}^{-1}(B)+E)))\to S_{1})

and
D^{Z}=\varphi_{1}^{-1}(\psi_{1}^{-1}(B)+E).

Moreover, each
(S,B)
as above gives a semi-contact projective snc pair
(Z,D^{Z})
with two good elementary log contractions.

Proof.

Let
\varphi_{3}:Z\to S
be the good log contraction of
(Z,D^{Z})
contracting the extremal face spanned by the rays corresponding to
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
. There exist morphisms
\psi_{1}:S_{1}\to S
and
\psi_{2}:S_{2}\to S
such that
\varphi_{3}=\psi_{1}\circ\varphi_{1}=\psi_{2}\circ\varphi_{2}
.
Theorem
5.14
describes the structure of
\varphi_{1}
and it implies that
\varphi_{1}
is a projective bundle with fibers of dimension
\geq r
.
If
\dim S=0
then
(Z,D^{Z})
is log Fano, contradicting our assumption. So we can assume that
\dim S>0
. Then Proposition
5.5
implies that a general fiber of
\varphi_{3}
is a projective space. So a general fiber of
\psi_{1}
is trivial, i.e.,
\psi_{1}
is birational.
If
\varphi_{2}
is of fiber type then the same argument shows that
\psi_{2}
is birational. But then
general fibers of
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
coincide, which contradicts our assumption that they contract different extremal rays. So
\varphi_{2}
is birational. If
{\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi_{2})
is not contained in
{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D^{Z}
then
\varphi_{2}
is very good. Then Theorem
5.14
implies that

\varphi_{2}
is of fiber type, a contradiction. Therefore
{\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi_{2})\subset{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D^{Z}
. For any stratum
Z^{\prime}
of the stratification induced by
D^{Z}
, the divisor
-(K_{Z^{\prime}}+D^{Z^{\prime}})=-(K_{Z}+D^{Z})|_{Z^{\prime}}
is
\varphi_{2}|_{Z^{\prime}}
-ample. Let us choose a stratum
Z^{\prime}
for which
\varphi_{2}|_{Z^{\prime}}
is not an isomorphism on
U_{Z^{\prime}}
. Then
(Z^{\prime},D^{Z^{\prime}})
is semi-contact with two different good log contractions
\varphi_{1}|_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\to S_{1}^{\prime}=\varphi_{1}(Z^{\prime})
and
\varphi_{2}|_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\to S_{2}^{\prime}=\varphi_{2}(Z^{\prime})
. In fact, both of them are projective bundles by
Theorem
5.14
. Let us consider the log contraction

\varphi_{3}|_{Z^{\prime}}:Z^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}=\varphi_{3}(Z^{\prime})
. Since there are no curves contracted by both
\varphi_{1}|_{Z^{\prime}}
and
\varphi_{2}|_{Z^{\prime}}
and the fibers of these morphisms have dimension
\geq r
, we have
\dim Z^{\prime}=2r
and
\dim S^{\prime}=0
. But then we are in the previous case. So
(Z^{\prime},D^{Z^{\prime}})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r},0)
, and
\varphi_{1}|_{Z^{\prime}}
and
\varphi_{2}|_{Z^{\prime}}
correspond to projections onto both factors of the product. It follows that
\varphi_{2}
is divisorial and hence by
[
KMM87
, Proposition 5.1.6]
we have
Z^{\prime}={\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi_{2})
.

If
\dim S_{1}=r
then
S_{1}=S_{1}^{\prime}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
is contracted by
\psi_{1}
to a point as
\varphi_{3}
contracts
Z^{\prime}
to a point. This contradicts our assumption that
\dim S>0
.
So
\dim S_{1}=r+1
and by Theorem
5.14
there exists a simple normal crossing divisor
B_{1}
on
S_{1}
such that
Z\simeq{\mathbb{P}}(T_{S_{1}}(-\log B_{1}))
and
D^{Z}=\varphi_{1}^{-1}(B_{1})
. Moreover, since
D^{Z^{\prime}}=0
,
S_{1}^{\prime}
is a connected component of
B_{1}
.
In the following we write
E
for
S_{1}^{\prime}
.
By construction
\psi_{1}(E)
is a point
that we denote by
s_{0}
. Since

\dim N_{1}(S_{1}/S)=1
(this follows from
[
KMM87
, Lemma 3.2.5]
) and
-K_{E}=-(K_{S_{1}}+B_{1})|_{E}
is ample, the divisor
-(K_{S_{1}}+B_{1})
is
\psi_{1}
-ample. This shows that
\psi_{1}
is a good elementary log contraction of
(S_{1},B_{1})
. Therefore by
[
KMM87
, Proposition 5.1.6]
,
E
is equal to the exceptional locus of
\psi_{1}
. We also know that

T_{E}(-\log E)|_{E}=T_{S_{1}}(-\log B_{1})|_{E}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}.

In particular, the short exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{E}\to T_{S_{1}}(-\log E)|_{E}\to T_{E}\to 0

does not split. The class of this extension in
{\mathop{{\rm Ext\,}}}^{1}_{E}(T_{E},{\mathcal{O}}_{E})=H^{1}(\Omega_{E}^{1})

is equal to
c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{E}({E}))
, so the normal bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}({E})
is non-trivial.
Since a subvariety with ample normal bundle can not be contracted to a point by a non-constant morphism,

{\mathcal{O}}_{E}({E})
is not ample and hence
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(-E)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(m)
for some
m>0
.
Now Mori’s version of the Grauert–Hironaka–Rossi theorem (see
[
Mor82
, Lemma 3.33]
) implies that
the formal completion of
S_{1}
along
E
is isomorphic to the formal completion of the total space of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(-m)
along the zero section. Moreover, the contraction
\psi_{1}
in a neighbourhood of
s_{0}\in S
looks formally like contraction of the zero section. Thus
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{S,s_{0}}

is isomorphic to the completion of the cone over the
m
-th Veronese embedding of
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
at the vertex.
This shows that
(Z,D^{Z})
is of the described form.

Now let us assume that
(Z,D^{Z})
is an snc pair coming from some
(S,B)
as described in the theorem.
The fact that
(Z,D^{Z})
is semi-contact follows from Proposition
6.1
. Since
c_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{E}(E))\neq 0
, the sequence

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{E}\to T_{S_{1}}(-\log(\psi_{1}^{-1}(B)+E))|_{E}\to T_{E}\to 0

is non-split and hence
T_{S_{1}}(-\log(\psi_{1}^{-1}(B)+E))|_{E}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}
. Therefore

Z^{\prime}=\varphi_{1}^{-1}(E)\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
.
Since
C={\mathbb{P}}^{1}\times\{x_{0}\}
is not contracted by
\varphi_{1}
and
(K_{Z}+D^{Z})\cdot C=K_{Z^{\prime}}\cdot C=-(r+1)<0
,
the pair
(Z,D^{Z})
admits another good log contraction.
∎

Remark 7.4
.

If
E
is a divisor on a smooth projective variety and
(E,{\mathcal{O}}_{E}(-E))\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(m))
for some
m>0
then by Artin-Grauert–Fujiki’s theorem
E
can be contracted to a point. However, the contraction exists only in the category of either algebraic or analytic spaces. This is why to describe
(Z,D^{Z})
in the above theorem we start with a projective log pair
(S,B)
and not with the snc pair
(S_{1},B_{1})
.

Remark 7.5
.

Note that in the above theorem
(S^{{\mathop{\rm an}}},s_{0})
is analytically isomorphic to the germ of the cone over the
m
-th Veronese embedding of
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
at the vertex. This immediately follows from Grauert’s criterion
[
Gra62
]
, whose assumptions are trivially satisfied. The only reason for the above formulation of Theorem
7.3
is avoiding analytic methods when dealing with algebraic varieties.

Example 7.6
.

Here we analyse the map
\psi_{2}:S_{2}\to S
induced by the second contraction in Theorem
7.3
.
By Theorem
5.14
the restriction of the second good elementary log contraction
\varphi_{2}:Z\to S_{2}
to
Z^{\prime}={\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi_{2})
is isomorphic to the projection of
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
onto the second factor. Therefore all reduced fibers of
\psi_{2}
are isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}
. Note also that over
S\backslash\{s_{0}\}
,
\psi_{2}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}(T_{S}(-\log B))
. Let
{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
be the unique reflexive extension of the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}(T_{S}(-\log B)|_{S\backslash\{s_{0}\}})}(1)
. By
[
Har80
, Corollary 1.7]
, the sheaf
\psi_{2*}{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
is reflexive.
So
\psi_{2*}{\mathcal{L}}_{2}\simeq T_{S}(-\log B)
as both sheaves are reflexive and isomorphic over
S\backslash\{s_{0}\}
. Since
S
is klt, the sheaf
T_{S}(-\log B)
(isomorphic to
T_{S}
at
s_{0}
) is not locally free at
s_{0}
unless
S
is smooth at
s_{0}
(see
[
GKKP11
, Theorem 6.1]
), which is equivalent to
m=1
. So
\psi_{2}
is a projective bundle if and only if
m=1
. For
m>1
this shows that Fujita’s theorem
[
Fuj87
, Lemma 2.12]
fails for even mildly singular varieties (note that
S_{2}
is klt as the pair
(S_{2},\varphi_{2*}(D^{Z}))
is dlt). The problem here is that the fiber of
\psi_{2}
over
s_{0}
is non-reduced.

For
m=1
we have
(S_{2}\to S)\simeq({\mathbb{P}}(T_{S}(-\log B))\to S)
and this example shows that
\varphi_{2}
is the blow up of the fiber of this projectivization over
s_{0}
.

Proposition
7.1
and Theorem
7.3
imply the following corollary:

Corollary 7.7
.

Let
(Z,D^{Z})
be a semi-contact projective snc pair that admits two good elementary log contractions

\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
. Assume that
{\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi_{1})\not\subset{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D^{Z}
.
Then either
(Z,D^{Z})
is contact or
(Z,D^{Z})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r},0)
.

Proof.

Assume first that
(Z,D^{Z})
is log Fano. Since
(Z,D^{Z})
admits two distinct elementary log contractions,
we have
b_{2}(Z)\geq 2
. So we can apply Proposition
7.1
. In the first two cases the pair
(Z,D^{Z})
is contact and in the third one we have
(Z,D^{Z})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{r},0)
.

Now let us assume that
(Z,D^{Z})
is not log Fano. Then by Theorem
7.3

there exist a projective variety
T_{1}
and a simple normal crossing divisor
B_{1}
(equal to
\psi_{1}^{-1}(B)+E

from the theorem) such that

(\varphi_{1}\colon Z\longrightarrow T_{1})\simeq\left(\mathbb{P}\bigl(T_{T_{1}}(-\log B_{1}\bigr)\longrightarrow T_{1}\right)

and

D^{Z}=\varphi_{1}^{-1}(B_{1}).

Therefore
(Z,D^{Z})
is a standard contact snc pair of the form considered
in Proposition
6.1
.
∎

Appendix A
Appendix: Semi-contact snc pairs in small dimensions

In this section we try to classify low dimensional semi-contact snc pairs.
In the whole section
(X,D)
is a semi-contact projective snc pair
with a special contact Lie algebroid
({\mathbb{L}},{\mathcal{L}})
of rank
(2r+1)
.
Let us first note the following simple corollary of our results:

Lemma A.1
.

If
\dim X=r
and
(K_{X}+D)
is not nef then
(X,D)\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{r},0)
. Moreover,
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}^{\oplus r}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}(1)^{\oplus(r+1)}
or
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}^{\oplus(r+1)}\oplus T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}}
.

Proof.

If
b_{2}(X)=1
then the assertion follows from Proposition
4.7
. So we can assume that
b_{2}(X)>1
. Then
D=0
again by Proposition
4.7
.
Since
K_{X}
is not nef, the cone theorem provides an elementary
K_{X}
-negative contraction

\varphi:X\to Y
with
\dim Y>0
. Then Theorem
5.14
implies that

X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}_{Y}({\mathcal{E}})
for a rank
r+1
vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
on
Y
. Hence
\dim X=\dim Y+r>r
, a contradiction.
∎

A.1
Dimension
1

In case
\dim X=1
the following lemma gives a full classification of semi-contact snc pairs.

Lemma A.2
.

If
\dim X=1
then
r=1
and one of the following occurs:

1.

X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
and
D
consists of
2
points,

2.

X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
and
D=0
,

3.

X
is an elliptic curve and
D=0
.

Proof.

Equality
r=1
follows from Lemma
4.9
. Lemma
4.13
implies that
h^{0}({\mathcal{L}})>0
and hence
\deg(K_{X}+D)\leq 0
. Since
-(K_{X}+D)=(r+1)c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
, we see that
(X,D)
is as above.

If
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
and
D=0
then we are in ituation of Lemma
A.1
.

If
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
and
D\neq 0
then
{\mathcal{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
and hence
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus 3}
.
Similarly, if
X
is an elliptic curve then
{\mathcal{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
.
In both cases existence a semi-contact structure on
{\mathbb{L}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\oplus 3}
follows from Example
4.15
.
∎

A.2
Dimension
2

Assume that
\dim X=2
. Then by Lemma
4.9
we have either
r=1
or
r=2
.

If
K_{X}+D
is pseudoeffective then
K_{X}+D\sim 0
. By Example
4.15
such semi-contact structures really occur both for
r=1
and
r=2
on compactifications of semi-abelian surfaces (as
X
one can even take an abelian surface with
D=0
).

If
K_{X}+D
is not pseudoeffective and
r=2
then we can use Lemma
A.1
. So in the following we can assume that
K_{X}+D
is not pseudoeffective and
r=1
.

If
b_{2}(X)=1
then Proposition
4.7
implies that
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{2}
.
D
is a hyperplane and
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2}}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2}}(1)^{\oplus 2}
.

If
b_{2}(X)>1
then
(X,D)
admits a good elementary log contraction
\varphi:X\to Y
with
\dim Y>0
.

If
\varphi
is of fiber type then by Theorem
5.14
,
r=1
,
Y
is a smooth projective curve and there exists a reduced divisor
B
on
Y
such that
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
,
D=\varphi^{-1}(B)
and
we have

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\to{\mathcal{E}}\to T_{Y}(-B)\to 0.

If
\varphi
is divisorial then
Z={\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi)\subset{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D
. So
Z
is an irreducible component of
D
and
(Z,D^{Z})
is semi-contact. But
-(K_{X}+D)
is
\varphi
-ample, so
-(K_{Z}+D^{Z})=-(K_{X}+D)|_{Z}
is ample. Hence
(Z,D^{Z})\simeq({\mathbb{P}}^{1},0)
by Lemma
A.2
. This shows that the image
D^{Y}
of the divisor
(D-Z)
is disjoint from the point
y_{0}=\varphi(Z)
. Note that if we write

K_{X}+D=\varphi^{*}(K_{Y}+D^{Y})+\alpha Z

then
\alpha=-2/Z^{2}>0
. So
K_{Y}+D^{Y}
is not pseudoeffective. Unfortunately,
Y
can be singular at
y_{0}
so we cannot immediately run an inductive procedure.

A.3
Dimension
3

Assume that
\dim X=3
. Then by Lemma
4.9
we have
1\leq r\leq 3
.

If
K_{X}+D
is pseudoeffective then by Corollary
4.14
we have
r>1
and

K_{X}+D\sim 0
. By Example
4.15

such semi-contact structures occur for both
r=2
and
r=3
on compactifications of semi-abelian
3
-folds.

Lemma A.3
.

If
K_{X}+D
is not pseudoeffective and
b_{2}(X)=1
then
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{3}
and one of the following cases occurs:

1.

r=1
,
D=0
and
{\mathbb{L}}=T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}
,

2.

r=1
,
D
is a sum of
2
hyperplanes, and
{\mathbb{L}}=T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}(-\log D)\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}(1)^{\oplus 2}
,

3.

r=2
,
D
is a hyperplane and
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}^{\oplus 2}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}(1)^{\oplus 3}
,

4.

r=3
,
D=0
and
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}^{\oplus 3}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}(1)^{\oplus 4}
,

5.

r=3
,
D=0
and
{\mathbb{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}^{\oplus 4}\oplus T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}
.

Proof.

If
r\geq 2
the assertion follows from Proposition
4.7
.
So we can assume that
r=1
. Then
(X,D)
is a contact log Fano variety with
-(K_{X}+D)=2c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
.
If
D=0
then it is well-known that
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{3}
(see
[
BKK22
, 2.1]
for the history of this assertion). So in the following we assume that
D\neq 0
.
Since
D=ac_{1}({\mathcal{L}})
for some
a>0
, the Kobayashi–Ochiai
theorem (see
[
Kol96
, Chapter V, Theorem 1.11]
) implies that
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{3}
or
X
is isomorphic to a
3
-dimensional quadric
Q^{3}
in
{\mathbb{P}}^{4}
. In the first case
a=2
and
D
is either a sum of two hyperplanes or
D
is a smooth quadric. In the first case
(X,D)
is contact.

If
D
is a smooth quadric then
{\mathcal{L}}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}(1)
and existence of a contact structure implies existence of a surjective map
T_{X}(-\log D)\to{\mathcal{L}}
with kernel
{\mathcal{F}}
.
In particular, since
{\mathcal{F}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}^{-1}
is a rank
2
vector bundle, we have

\int_{X}c_{3}((T_{X}(-\log D))\otimes{\mathcal{L}}^{-1})=\int_{X}c_{3}({\mathcal{F}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}^{-1})=0.

But a short computation using the short exact sequence

0\to T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}(-\log D)\to T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{D}(D)\to 0

shows that
\int_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}c_{3}((T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{3}}(-\log D))\otimes{\mathcal{L}}^{-1})=-1\neq 0
, a contradiction.

If
X\simeq Q^{3}
then
a=1
and hence
D
is isomorphic to a smooth
2
-dimensional quadric in
{\mathbb{P}}^{3}
. The proof is this case is similar as above. Namely, if
{\mathcal{F}}
is the kernel of
T_{X}(-\log D)\to{\mathcal{L}}
then

\int_{X}c_{3}((T_{X}(-\log D))\otimes{\mathcal{L}}^{-1})=\int_{X}c_{3}({\mathcal{F}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}^{-1})=0.

Now short exact sequences

0\to T_{Q^{3}}(-\log D)\to T_{Q^{3}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{D}(D)\to 0

and

0\to T_{Q^{3}}\to T_{{\mathbb{P}}^{4}}|_{Q^{3}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Q^{3}}(2)\to 0

allow us to compute
\int_{Q^{3}}c_{3}((T_{Q^{3}}(-\log D))\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{Q^{3}}(-1))=-2
, a contradiction.
∎

Now let us assume that
b_{2}(X)>1
and
K_{X}+D
is not pseudoeffective. Then the pair
(X,D)
admits a good elementary log contraction
\varphi:X\to Y
with
\dim Y>0
. Note that in this case
r=1
or
r=2
by Lemma
A.1
.

If
\varphi
is of fiber type then by Theorem
5.14
,
X\simeq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
for some vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
and there exists a simple normal crossing divisor
B
on
Y

such that
D^{v}=\varphi^{-1}(B)
and one of the following happens:

1.

r=1
,
\dim Y=1
and there exists a line bundle
{\mathcal{M}}
on
Y
such that

0\to{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{D}}^{\leq 1}_{T_{Y}(-\log B)}({\mathcal{M}})\to 0

and
D^{h}\simeq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{D}}^{\leq 1}_{T_{Y}(-\log B)}({\mathcal{M}}))
,

2.

r=1
,
\dim Y=2
,
D^{h}=0
and
{\mathcal{E}}\simeq T_{Y}(-\log B)
,

3.

r=2
,
\dim Y=1
,
D^{h}=0
and

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}^{\oplus 2}\to{\mathcal{E}}\to T_{Y}(-\log B)\to 0.

If
\varphi
is of divisorial type then the exceptional divisor
Z={\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi)
is contained in
D
, so it is an irreducible component of
D
. It follows that
(Z,D^{Z})
is semi-contact and
\varphi|_{Z}
is a good log contraction of fiber type. If
\dim\varphi(Z)=0
then by classification in the
2
-dimensional case and by Proposition
7.1
we have the following possibilities:

1.

r=2
,
Z\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{2}
and
D^{Z}=0
,

2.

r=1
,
Z\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{2}
and
D^{Z}
is a line (so
D
has another irreducible component),

3.

r=1
,
Z\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\times{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
and
D^{Z}=0
.

If
\dim\varphi(Z)=1
then
\varphi|_{Z}
is elementary (e.g., by Proposition
7.1
and Theorem
7.3
). So
as in the
2
-dimensional case
r=1
,
T=\varphi(Z)
is a smooth projective curve and there exists a reduced divisor
B
on
T
such that
Z\simeq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
(over
T
),
D^{Z}=(\varphi|_{Z})^{-1}(B)
and
we have

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{T}\to{\mathcal{E}}\to T_{T}(-B)\to 0.

If
\varphi
is small then
{\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi)\subset{\mathop{{\rm Supp\,}}}D
. Let
Z
be an irreducible component of
D
,
which contains a curve contracted by
\varphi
. The pair
(Z,D^{Z})
is semi-contact and
\varphi|_{Z}
is a good
divisorial log contraction of this pair (possibly non-elementary).
Then
\varphi|_{Z}
factors through an elementary divisorial contraction of
(Z,D^{Z})
. So from the
2
-dimensional case this we know that
{\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi|_{Z})
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
and it does not intersect any other component of
D
. Hence
{\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi)
is a disjoint union of numerically equivalent
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
’s.
Each connected component
C\simeq{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
of
{\mathop{{\rm Exc\,}}}(\varphi)
is an intersection of two irreducible components
Z
and
Z^{\prime}
of
D
.
Note that the conormal bundle
N^{*}_{C/X}
is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(-Z)\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{C}(-Z^{\prime})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-a)\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}}(-b)
, where
a=-(C.C)_{Z}>0
and
b=-(C.C)_{Z^{\prime}}>0
.

Appendix B
Appendix: Compactification of a
1
-jet bundle to a b-contact manifold

In this appendix we work in the category of smooth real manifolds and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-functions.
With suitable modifications, one can replace this with real analytic or complex analytic (holomorphic) objects. Before explaining analogue of our construction in the real case, let us recall some basic notions of b-geometry. Let
(M,Z)
be a b-manifold, i.e., a smooth real manifold
M
with the boundary hypersurface
Z
, called the critical set
(see
[
Mel93
, 2.1]
for a precise definition). Then one defines a b-tangent bundle
\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 9.01083pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 11.15555pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}TM
(see
[
Mel93
, Lemma 2.5]
) and its dual, a b-cotangent bundle
\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 10.94142pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 13.58614pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}T^{*}M=(\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 9.01083pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 11.15555pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}TM)^{*}
.

Let
(M,Z)
be a b-manifold of dimension
(2r+1)
. In
[
MO23
]
a
b-contact structure
on
(M,Z)
is defined as a corank
1
subbundle
\xi
of the b-tangent bundle (such
\xi
is called a
corank
1
b-distribution
) given by the kernel of a globally defined b-
1
-form
\alpha\in\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\Omega}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\Omega}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.83614pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\Omega}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.65285pt\mathopen{\vphantom{\Omega}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}\Omega^{1}(M)
such that
\alpha\wedge(d\alpha)^{\wedge r}

is nowhere vanishing as a section of
\bigwedge^{2r+1}(\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 10.94142pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 13.58614pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}T^{*}M)
. Note that in this definition
\xi
is necessarily coorientable, i.e., the normal bundle
\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 10.94142pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 13.58614pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}T^{*}M/\xi
is trivial. But from our point of view
the following definition is more natural:

Definition B.1
.

A
b-contact structure
on the b-manifold
(M,Z)
is a corank
1
b-distribution
\xi\subset\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 9.01083pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 11.15555pt\mathopen{\vphantom{TM}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}TM
such that for every point
p\in M
there exists an open neighbourhod of
p
on which
\xi
is given by the kernel of a local b-
1
-form
\alpha
such that
\alpha\wedge(d\alpha)^{\wedge r}
does not vanish at
p
.

It is well known that the bundle of contact elements of
M
is the projectivized cotangent bundle
and it carries a natural contact structure (see, e.g.,
[
Arn89
, Appendix 4 D]
or
[
Gei08
, 1.2]
). Note that here we use traditional in real geometry convention that the projectivized bundle is defined by lines in the fibers of the bundle and not by hyperplanes as in the main body of the paper (in this case we talked about projectivizations rather than projectivized bundles).
There is also an analogous construction of the manifold of oriented contact elements that in the case of a Riemannian manifold is isomorphic to the unit sphere bundle in
T^{*}M
(see, e.g.,
[
MS98
, Example 3.45]
). Both these structures come from the homogeneous symplectic structure on the cotangent bundle
T^{*}M
(see
[
KS90
, A.2]
and
[
Arn89
, Appendix 4 E]
).
Let us recall that the canonical contact structure on the projectivised cotangent bundle does not need to be coorientable
(see, e.g.,
[
Gei08
, Examples 1.2.4 and 2.1.11]
). This is the main reason why we extend the definition of a b-contact structure.

For any b-manifold
(M,Z)
there is also an analogous construction of a canonical contact structure on the projectivization of the b-cotangent bundle
\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 10.94142pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 13.58614pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T^{*}M}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}T^{*}M
. An analogue of the construction for the unit sphere bundle in
\mathchoice{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 4.01666pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 5.92233pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}{\mathop{}\kern 6.8881pt\mathopen{\vphantom{T}}^{\mathmakebox[0pt][l]{\mathmakebox[r]{b}}}}T^{*}M
(presumably after fixing an auxiliary Riemannian metric) is described in
[
BDM+19
, Section 6, Example 2]
and
[
MO23
, Example 8.4]
.

Here we describe a different b-contact manifold, which is a fibration with compact fibers over a given smooth (possibly compact) manifold
M
. This is an easy analogue of Proposition
6.3
(in case of one line bundle and
B=0
). To describe the construction we need to fix a line bundle
L\to M
(possibly trivial). It is well known that the vector bundle
J^{1}(L)\to M
of first jets of sections of
L
carries a natural contact structure (see
[
MS98
, Example 3.44]
for the case of the trivial line bundle
L\to M
). Possibly the best way to describe this contact structure is to note that the canonical symplectic structure on the cotangent bundle of the complement
(L^{*})^{\times}
of the zero section in
L^{*}
descends to a contact structure on the quotient

T^{*}((L^{*})^{\times})/{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}
(this point of view on contact structures was used already in
[
LeB95
]
; cf. the proof of Proposition
3.9
). Now
J^{1}(L)
can be canonically identified with this contact manifold
T^{*}((L^{*})^{\times})/{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}
.
Our aim is to (relatively) compactify this contact manifold to a b-contact manifold. The b-compactification is given by the projectivized bundle
{\mathbf{P}}(J^{1}(L)\times{\mathbb{R}})\to M
with the critical set
Z
given by the added hypersurface at infinity.
We describe this
b
-contact structure in local coordinates.

Let
(q^{1},...,q^{n})
be local coordinates for
M
over some open subset over which we fix a local trivialization of
L
. Then

J^{1}(L)
has local coordinates
(q^{1},...,q^{n},p_{1},...,p_{n},t)
with standard notation, and on
J^{1}(L)\times{\mathbb{R}}
we have an additional coordinate
z
corresponding to the second factor of the product. Then on
J^{1}(L)\times{\mathbb{R}}
with a b-manifold structure given by the critical set
z=0
, we can define a b-
1
-form by

t\frac{dz}{z}-dt+\sum p_{i}dq^{i}.

This form is homogeneous with respect to the natural
{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}
-action on fiber coordinates over
M
(see the proof of Proposition
6.3
) and it is non-vanishing outside of the zero section. So it gives rise to a contact b-
1
-form on
{\mathbf{P}}(J^{1}(L)\times{\mathbb{R}})
. The proof that the form does not depend on the choice of local coordinates is left to the reader.
